# Experiments ledger

**Keep `experiments.json` in step:** whenever you add an experiment here or change its status or result, update its entry in `data/orchestrator/experiments.json` in the same step (plain language, Toronto times). Discord's `#experiments` channel shows that file.

One experiment at a time per subsystem: **training** (self-play/training),
**live** (search/live play), **data**. Times UTC unless marked Toronto.

Entries E1-E3 were started by the owner before the orchestrator existed; the
first orchestrator run (2026-10-13 04:10Z) recorded them here retroactively.

## E1 — training: strict gate + human starts + aux targets + 400 sims
- **Subsystem:** training. **Status:** done, KEPT (verdict 11:20Z Oct 13, round 26).
  **Started:** round 22 (human
  starts 25%, aux weight 0.1, strict gate from round 22's match, ~01:54Z
  Oct 13); round 23 adds 400 simulations / 3500 games (02:17Z Oct 13).
- **Hypothesis:** stronger policy targets (400 sims) and human-start
  positions give nets that beat round 19 clearly (SPRT 50 vs 54%) and lower
  live value MSE, especially in the opening.
- **Metric:** promotion under the strict gate; live value MSE (now 0.646,
  opening 0.951) on data/human/holdout/value.
- **Decision point:** rounds 23-26 (four strict matches). If none promotes
  and live MSE doesn't fall below ~0.63, the 400-sim change isn't paying;
  consider reverting to 160 sims / 5000 games, or a different lever.
- **Revert:** `SELFPLAY_SIMULATIONS=160 SELFPLAY_GAMES=5000` in train.sh
  defaults, `--human-start-rate 0`, then safe_restart_training.sh.
- **Notes:** round 22 (160 sims, human starts): 50.4% at 1400, kept.
  Round 23 (first with 400 sims): 49.9% over 900 games (449-451), kept; live MSE
  0.637 (opening 0.949, middle 0.574, late 0.495), on the same 7657 positions as
  rounds 20-22 (0.642-0.646). That's a small drop, likely noise.
  1 of 4 strict matches done.
- **Note (analysis 2026-10-13):** opening live MSE is close to irreducible. The
  best constant scores ~0.974 against the net's 0.945, so judge on middle and late MSE.
  The "0.40 self-play" figure is against 0.5·z + 0.5·search value, so it isn't
  comparable.
- **Note (05:10Z Oct 13), for the round-26 decision:** each candidate is the
  best net (round 19) plus 3 epochs over a 22-folder window of 28531 games, of
  which one round of 400-sim data is ~3500 (~12%). The new targets are a small
  share of what each candidate sees, which by itself would explain ~50%
  matches. If E1 fails, the next lever to consider is a shorter window or
  upweighting the newest rounds, not just reverting sims.
- **Note (06:10Z Oct 13):** round 24 (the 2nd 400-sim round, 1st with E3 data)
  **passed the strict SPRT**: 488-412, 54.2% ± 3.2% over 900 games. Live MSE
  0.638 (opening 0.949 / middle 0.574 / late 0.487) on the grown holdout (9525
  positions, 242 games), so it isn't directly comparable with 0.637 / 7657.
  Next: guardrails (benchmark vs AB:2 at 400 games, then the anchor: since no net
  has an anchor score yet, round 19 plays 2000 games vs round 13 first, then round
  24 plays 2000). That's roughly 70-80 min. The verdict is for the 07:10Z or 08:10Z run.
  If round 24 promotes, E1 counts as a success on its primary metric. Then
  watch whether live play improves once the bots pick up round 24 (bot nets
  update from best.npz).
- **Note (07:10Z Oct 13): round 24 PROMOTED** at 06:58Z. Guardrails: benchmark
  0.950 (= round 19); anchor against round 13: round 24 53.4% vs round 19 52.6%
  (2000 games each, ±2.1%, so +0.8 pts, inside the noise but no drop); live MSE
  0.638. Gauntlet: 73% vs sp1 rounds 16 and 8. The primary metric is met. The verdict at
  round 26 is "success" unless 25/26 show a regression. The bots switch between games:
  bot3 was first on round 24 at ~07:10Z. Live follow-up: bot3/bot4 ranked
  win rate on round 24 against round 19's ~57% (bot3+bot4 last 24 h,
  183-137). It needs >=200 round-24 games before reading anything.
  Lever under research for after E1: training-window recency (a subagent at 07:12Z).
- **Note (08:10Z Oct 13):** round 25 self-play since 07:06Z (832/3500 games at 07:51Z,
  the early rate understates it; round 24's self-play took 86 min, so the match is ~08:45-09:00Z). Live round 24 ranked: 12-6 over 18 games across bot3-6.
  Far too few to read.
- **Note (09:10Z Oct 13):** round 25 (the first round based on round 24) was **kept**: 50.5% over 1300
  games (SPRT accepted H0). Live MSE 0.635 (0.948 / 0.565 / 0.495) on a holdout
  grown to 10005 positions, so it isn't comparable with round 24's 0.638 / 9525. That's not a regression, just
  no second gain. 3 of 4 strict matches done (1 pass). Round 26 self-play started
  09:09Z; its match is ~10:45-11:10Z. Live round 24, ranked: 21-14 over 35 games (60%,
  ±16%). Too few.
- **Note (10:10Z Oct 13):** round 26 still in self-play. Live round 24 ranked: 30-24 over 54
  (56%, ±13%; bot3 9-5, bot4 8-6, bot5 7-6, bot6 6-7). Too few. E4 armed to begin
  at round 27; round 26 itself trains under E1's settings.
- **VERDICT (11:20Z Oct 13): keep, a partial success.** Round 26 (trained on 22 folders, i.e. the old
  settings, so it's clean; the restart came at 10:48:40Z, during the match) was **kept**: 864-836, 50.8% ± 2.2%
  over 1700 games vs round 24. Live MSE 0.638 (0.948 / 0.573 / 0.492) on 10085
  positions. Over the 4 strict matches (rounds 23-26): 1 promotion (round 24, 54.2%), scores
  49.9 / 54.2 / 50.5 / 50.8. Live MSE stayed at 0.635-0.638, so it didn't reach the
  <0.63 target. The middle/late phases are flat too. Keep 400 sims + human starts (no evidence
  they hurt, and they gave the one promotion). The live-MSE gap was not moved by this lever. The
  next training lever is E4 (window), already deployed from round 27.
  Live round 24 ranked at 11:10Z: 46-30 / 76 (61% ± 11%; bot3 14-6, bot4 11-9,
  bot5 11-7, bot6 10-8). Still too few to compare with round 19.

## E4 — training: a shorter, fresher window
- **Subsystem:** training. **Status:** done, KEPT (verdict Oct 14 01:13Z); ran from round 27 (round 27 started 11:16:48Z
  Oct 13; colonist-train restarted with E4 at 10:48:40Z, during round 26's match; round 26's
  training line read 22 folders, so it's clean). Check round 27's "training on N data
  folders" (expect ~10) at ~12:45Z. Earlier status: deploying (10:25Z Oct 13). train.sh edited and
  committed; safe_restart_training.sh armed, so colonist-train restarts when round 26's
  promotion match begins (~10:35-10:50Z). Round 26 was trained under the old settings
  (its training ends before the restart), so E1's verdict stays clean; round 27 is the first
  E4 round. Risk: a watchdog restart before then would apply E4 to round 26's training
  (restarts were 0; check `restarted` in data/orchestrator/jobs/restart-training.log
  and the "training on N data folders" line for round 26 = 22, round 27 should be ~10:
  6 g1 rounds + 4 human copies).
- **Decision point:** rounds 27-30 (four strict matches).
- **Finding (07:15Z Oct 13, research subagent; checked against train.sh):** the
  window is 8 g1 rounds, plus sp1 rounds 13-16 (old MLP-era searches, ~28% of
  games), plus human value ×10 (train.sh:33,37,42). The GPU loads a random 4M-row subset
  of ~9.5M rows (loop.py:258-261, train.py:140-155). The 400-sim data is ~10%
  of the window. KataGo's window formula gives ~3M rows here, about 6-7 rounds.
- **Change:** train.sh:37 `--window 6`; train.sh:42 `--extra-data ${human#,}` (drop
  sp1); `HUMAN_REPEAT=4` (keeps the human share ~3%).
- **Hypothesis:** with 400-sim targets at ~78% of the window, and no stale sp1 policy, the strict-gate pass
  rate goes up.
- **Metric:** strict passes / mean score over rounds 27-30 vs 22-26; live MSE
  middle/late must not rise more than 0.01; anchor >= round 24's 53.4%; watch the val value
  loss for overfitting (each row is now seen ~18×, up from ~10×).
- **Revert:** restore `--window 8`, the sp1 extra-data and the HUMAN_REPEAT default of 10.
- **Next lever if flat:** value mix 0.5 → 0.75 (Lc0 q_ratio). It needs a `--value-mix`
  flag in loop.py.

## E2 — live: human opponent model at the opponent's nodes (Petra4620 / bot5)
- **Subsystem:** live. **Status:** running. **Started:** Oct 12 22:13
  Toronto (02:13Z Oct 13), bot5 only, OPPONENT_MODEL + OPPONENT_MIX (see the
  bot VM's accounts/bot5.env; example default 0.5).
- **Hypothesis:** modelling people's moves (not the bot's own policy) at the
  opponent's nodes makes search exploit human tendencies and resist repeat
  opponents; ranked win rate rises.
- **Metric:** bot5 ranked win rate with the model vs bot5 before it (52-29,
  64%) and vs bot3/bot4 over the same hours (control). Confound: bot5 is a
  new account, its rating (1899) is climbing, so opponents get harder; the
  same-hours control matters more than bot5's own history.
- **Decision point:** extended Oct 14 to >=400 games with the model; the first >=150-game review was inconclusive. Revert if
  clearly below the control (outside the ±8% interval).
- **Revert:** remove OPPONENT_MODEL/OPPONENT_MIX from bot5's env file, then
  safe_restart_account.sh bot5.
- **Log:** 04:10Z Oct 13: 7-4 with model. Far too few.
  05:10Z Oct 13: 8-6 with model (57%, ±26%). Control over the last 24 h:
  bot3 93-74 (56%), bot4 98-67 (59%). No signal yet.
  06:10Z Oct 13: 13-7 with model (65%, ±21%). Control over the last 24 h: bot3 89-74
  (55%), bot4 95-65 (59%). Inside the noise. Caveat: if round 24 promotes, all
  accounts switch nets at the same time, so the bot3/bot4 control stays valid.
  bot6 (hersh3499, Gold, 13-1) is not a control: its rating is far lower.
  07:10Z Oct 13: 15-10 with model (60%, ±19%). Control over the last 24 h: bot3 88-73
  (55%), bot4 95-64 (60%). Inside the noise. From ~07:10Z all accounts move
  from round 19 to round 24 together, so the control stays same-net.
  08:10Z Oct 13: 19-11 with model (63%, ±17%), 30 of 150 games. Control over the last 24 h: bot3
  81-74 (52%), bot4 93-62 (60%). Inside the noise.
  09:10Z Oct 13: 22-12 with model (65%, ±16%), 34 of 150. Control over the last 24 h: bot3 78-74
  (51%), bot4 91-60 (60%). Inside the noise.
  10:10Z Oct 13: 23-16 with model (59%, ±15%), 39 of 150. Control over the last 24 h: bot3 78-72
  (52%), bot4 89-59 (60%). Inside the noise.
  11:10Z Oct 13: 27-17 with model (61%, ±14%), 44 of 150. Control over the last 24 h: bot3 80-68
  (54%), bot4 86-59 (59%). Inside the noise. Petra 1976 (rating still climbing).

## E3 — data: fresh human data every round
- **Subsystem:** data. **Status:** done, KEPT as infrastructure (verdict Oct 14 01:13Z; strength benefit unproven). **Started:** round 24 (the
  loop's --before-round runs scripts/vm/human_dataset.sh).
- **Hypothesis:** adding the newest live games every round (value rows,
  starts, opponent-model refresh) keeps the live value error falling.
- **Metric:** live value MSE trend; opponent-model held-out accuracy.
- **Decision point:** rounds 24-27. Caveat: the holdout grows too, so MSE
  numbers across rounds aren't on identical positions once it updates.
- **Revert:** drop `--before-round` from train.sh.
- **Log:** round 24's before-round step ran at 04:14Z (1m47s). Opponent-model
  refresh at 04:40Z replaced the model: held-out cross-entropy 0.977 vs 0.988
  (better), accuracy 64.47% vs 64.52% (flat), 7261 rows.
  06:10Z: the live holdout grew from 7657 positions / 194 games to 9525 / 242.
  Round 24 scores 0.638 on it. From now on, compare MSE only between nets on the
  same holdout (the guardrail does this: it re-scores the best net).
  Round 25's refresh (07:32Z, initialised from round 24's best.npz) replaced the model again:
  held-out CE 0.972 vs 0.978, accuracy 64.9% vs 64.5% (7584 rows). That's the second
  small gain in a row.
  Round 26's refresh (09:37Z) replaced the model, though its held-out CE is slightly WORSE (0.9716 vs
  0.9699) and accuracy +0.1 pt (65.11 vs 65.02%, 7656 rows). That's by design:
  opponent_model.py replaces when CE is within --tolerance. A +0.0017 CE is negligible. E3's decision is at round 27: live MSE 0.635-0.638
  on the growing holdout, so no clear downward trend yet.

## E5 — training: raise human-game starts to 35%
- **Subsystem:** self-play/training. **Status:** CLOSED Oct 15, causal benefit inconclusive; 35% retained as current baseline, not a demonstrated gain. Follow-up E7 completed without a decisive advantage. **Owner request:** 13 Oct 2026.
- **Hypothesis:** more search-generated policy and value targets from positions reached in human games improve live-game decisions, especially early-game calibration.
- **Change:** `scripts/vm/train.sh` launches `loop.py` with `--human-start-rate 0.35` instead of its implicit 0.25 default. Keep `HUMAN_REPEAT=4`, 3,500 games, model, search settings, and promotion guardrails fixed.
- **Metric:** compare new candidates with the incumbent on the same held-out human-game set, including opening value MSE; require the existing strict promotion gate, anchor and benchmark guardrails. Track live ranked win rate and Elo against people after a promotion; do not declare success from fewer than about 400 live games.
- **Decision point:** initial rounds 32–34 yielded no promotion; extended through round 43 to let the six-round replay window fully turn over. At the next report, decide using rounds 38–43 and same-holdout calibration; if no promotion and no material calibration gain, revert to 25%. Record realized human-start share from shard metadata and compare with the intended 35%.
- **Revert:** set `HUMAN_START_RATE=0.25` (or restore the prior launch argument), then restart only at a safe round boundary.

## E6 — operations: balance self-play games by measured worker throughput
- **Subsystem:** self-play scheduling only; game seeds, moves, targets, search settings, process counts and evaluation are unchanged. **Status:** KEPT provisionally Oct 14: runtime benefit verified, shard metadata audit pending. Active from round 32.
- **Observed:** rounds 29–30: coordinator about 0.31 games/s, two four-process GCP workers about 0.18 each, four small workers about 0.09 each, PC (10 processes) about 0.19. The process-proportional split gave the PC 1004 games and faster four-process workers 448 each, leaving the PC as the end-of-round bottleneck.
- **Change:** `COLONIST_SELFPLAY_CAPACITIES` keyed by host with measured hundredths of games/s: local 31, the two four-process workers 18 each, the four small workers 9 each, PC 19. Largest-remainder shard allocation gives round 32 (3500 games): local 896; four-process workers 512 each; small workers 256 each; PC 556. All seed ranges remain consecutive and disjoint.
- **Metric:** compare round 32's last-shard time across workers and total self-play duration with rounds 29–30; verify exactly 3500 completed games, no gaps or overlaps. Recalibrate only between rounds if speeds change, especially with 35% human starts.
- **Revert:** remove `COLONIST_SELFPLAY_CAPACITIES` from `scripts/vm/workers.env`; the splitter then uses its prior process-based weights. Restart at a safe round boundary if the launch process needs to pick up code.

## E7 — isolated paired human-start rate test (Oct 14, 2026)
- **Subsystem:** self-play/training. **Status:** COMPLETE, inconclusive (reconciled Oct 15); production settings unchanged.
- **Hypothesis:** 35% human starts improves human-game calibration or match strength over 25% when all other inputs are held fixed.
- **Data:** two 3,500-game branches from round-40 best net (SHA-256 c3febf660ac176393e8ecc9bab3c8dfa5bcd13168679832081c8976d2f252172), same seeds 4043000000–4043003499, same frozen human starts (SHA-256 dd9edf10ccd60454b36f0139df2a030076d14aabc7bb7d1885396484c23a05f3), opponents, search budget and code. Only `--human-start-rate` differs. Local Mac runs, ignored data under `data/experiments/human-start-rate-20261014/`.
- **Pilot:** 128 seeds per arm completed. 25% branch used 35 human starts; 35% used 46. All 117 games with the same assignment had identical state trajectories and outcomes; one policy target had a 0.0000019 rounding difference.
- **Next:** after both 3,500-game branches complete, train from the same round-40 checkpoint with frozen rounds 38–42 and human value data (4 repeats), three epochs, seed 43, replacing only round-43 data by each branch. Compare on one fixed human holdout and in at least 1,000 seat-swapped head-to-head games. Do not promote solely from validation loss. Keep production and live bots unchanged until evidence is reviewed.
- **Decision:** keep 35% only if it wins the paired match clearly without worsening human-game calibration; otherwise revert the production rate to 25% at a safe round boundary. Isolated files can simply be deleted to revert this test.

## E10 — search: root win check (QUEUED, not deployed)
- **Subsystem:** search/live play (queued behind E2). Code is in mcts.py and off by default (`Search(win_check=False)`), so self-play and live play are unchanged.
- **Evidence:** the root's Gumbel top-k picks `considered=16` candidates by prior + noise only (mcts.py `steps`). A legal move that wins at once but has a low prior is never visited, at any simulation count. failure-set files show nets skipping immediate wins in 6/35 (targeted-starts) and 5 and 3 (search-mix) flagged positions at 100 sims. That count includes deferred wins, not only forfeited ones.
- **Change:** `winning_moves(game, color, actions)`: when actual VP >= target-2, each deterministic build or knight is copied and executed. Any move that wins is forced into the candidates and always chosen. The search still runs, so the training targets keep their form.
- **Hypothesis:** no forfeited winning turns. These are rare but decisive in close endgames (48% win rate beyond 90 turns).
- **Test plan:** (1) size the problem with bonus_audit `missed_immediate_win_turns` over the last 300 live captures. bonus_audit is not in the orchestrator allowlist (asked of the owner). (2) Overhead at most 5% think time. (3) 1000 seat-swapped games, round 40 with the check vs without, at 400 sims; the expected gain is small, so this mainly checks that nothing breaks. (4) One account for >=400 ranked games, after E2 closes.
- **Revert:** leave the flag off (the default). Nothing is deployed.

## E11 — training: a bigger net (RUNNING offline from Oct 16 13:12Z; production untouched)
- **Started Oct 16 13:12Z (orchestrator):** PC job `runs/e11/train.sh` (copy in data/orchestrator/jobs/e11-train.sh; log runs/e11/train.log on the PC; remote PID 155722). Holds the PC's gpu.lock (a trap removes it); expected done ~14:00Z, well before round 63's training (~15:15Z). If it overlaps, the loop trains on the coordinator CPU (slower, not broken).
- **Design:** both arms from round 56 (best), round 62's window (g1 rounds 57-62 + human value ×4), 4 epochs, lr 3e-4, sample 4M, max-positions 3M, seed 62. Control 32,64,3 (102,377 params); wide = `graph.widen()` 48,96,3 (function-preserving). Only size differs.
- **Metric / decision:** (1) val policy/value loss, wide vs control (a gap above epoch-to-epoch noise, ~0.003 policy); (2) same-holdout human MSE (loop's live scorer); (3) 1000+ seat-swapped games wide vs control at 200 sims, then at equal think time (measure eval cost first). Adopt only if (3) at equal time clearly favours wide (95% lower bound > 50%). Run the matches when CPUs are not needed by production, or distributed with the owner's OK.
- **Revert:** nothing deployed; delete nothing (runs/e11 on the PC is the artifact).

- **Log 13:45Z Oct 16:** control finished (val policy 0.8311, val value 0.3938, acc 0.7950 at epoch 4; epochs 1-2 ~600 s with the match using the PC's CPUs, 3-4 160 s). Wide arm started ~13:43Z.

- **Diagnosis 20:20Z Oct 16 (Mac Claude session, owner request; read-only, CPU on the PC, script /tmp/e11diag.py there):** on E11's own validation split (152k rows: 38k self-play with a search policy, 99k quick-search z-only, 16k human), round 56 → control → wide: policy CE 0.8352 / 0.8327 / 0.8325; target entropy 0.4794, so KL to the search policy 0.3557 / 0.3533 / 0.3530; value MSE 0.3991 / 0.3981 / 0.3979. Wide − control, paired per game: policy −0.0003 ± 0.00014, value −0.00001 ± 0.0006. **Both arms barely moved from round 56** (KL −0.0024), so E11 measured a short fine-tune, not net size. The wide net's new features ended with ~25% of the old features' outgoing weight (output heads ~10%): partly used. Value loss is mostly outcome noise (by game stage 0.62 early → 0.17 late; quick rows 0.474 vs target variance 0.976); the human rows got worse in both arms (0.525 → 0.529). The reducible gap is the policy KL of 0.35: the net is far from its search's policy, which is where a bigger, longer-trained net could gain.

### E11 original queue note
- **Hypothesis:** at ~98k params the net underfits; a widened round-40 net (graph.py `widen()`, e.g. 48,96,3 or 64,128,4) trained on the same window beats an unwidened control from the same data and seed.
- **Metric:** final train/val loss, same-holdout human MSE, 2000-game match vs round 40 at 100 sims and at equal think time (the bigger net costs maybe 2–4x per evaluation, an unmeasured estimate).
- **Run:** offline on the PC GPU between the loop's trainings (respect gpu.lock), so production is untouched. **Revert:** nothing to revert until it is adopted.

## E11b — training: bigger nets, trained long enough to use their size (RUNNING offline from Oct 16 ~21:00Z; production untouched)
- **Owner-approved Oct 16 ~5 pm Toronto** (Mac Claude session): "run E11b on the PC between the loop's trainings: 32,64,3 vs 48,96,3 vs 32,64,5, longer training at a higher learning rate on the same data".
- **Design:** three arms, all from round 56 (runs/e11/base.npz): control 32,64,3; wide 48,96,3 (runs/e11/wide.npz, E11's widened init); deep 32,64,5 (widen(): 2 extra residual message-passing rounds, near zero at start). Same data as E11 (g1 rounds 57-62 + human value ×4, max-positions 3M, seed 62), 12 epochs (was 4) at lr 1e-3 (was 3e-4), cosine, --ema 0.9997 like production, sample 4M. Only the size differs. net.npz = best validation epoch; log.csv per epoch.
- **Jobs:** controller on the coordinator data/orchestrator/jobs/e11b-run.sh (pid in e11b-run.pid, log e11b.log) starts one arm at a time on the PC only while runs/g1 state step is match or benchmark (training already done, the next one hours away), detached; each arm (runs/e11b/arm.sh on the PC, copy in jobs/e11b-arm.sh) holds gpu.lock and runs niced; log runs/e11b/train.log on the PC; an arm that fails is retried once. Expect ~35-50 min per arm on an idle PC, up to ~3× that while the match uses the PC's CPUs. If an arm still overlaps the loop's training, the loop trains on the coordinator CPU (slower, not broken). Stop: kill the controller pid, then the arm's train process on the PC (gpu.lock is removed by its trap).
- **Metric / decision:** (1) the E11 diagnostic on the shared validation split: policy KL and value MSE by row type, paired per game, against control-12 (and control-12 against E11's control, which says whether longer/hotter training alone helps production); (2) same-holdout human MSE; (3) if wide or deep beats control-12 by clearly more than noise (policy KL ≥ ~0.005), measure its evaluation cost, then 1000+ seat-swapped games vs control-12 at 200 sims and at equal think time. Adopt only if equal time clearly favours it (95% lower bound > 50%), with the owner's OK (it changes the production net size and the bots' speed).
- **Revert:** nothing deployed; delete nothing (runs/e11b on the PC is the artifact).

## E13 — self-play against a human-like opponent (planned, owner-approved Oct 16)
- Step 1 (running): retrain a stronger human opponent model (models/human-exp: variants A-E, longer training, LRs, policy-only, wider nets), scored on data/human/holdout/opponent.
- Step 2: a share of self-play games with one seat sampling moves from the human model (no search), so the main net learns to beat human-style play (cf. RL-DiL-piKL, Diplomacy). Start after E12 has a round of its own.
  - **Code landed 1a49eba (Oct 17 ~19:05Z), off by default, NOT deployed:** `selfplay --human-opponent <model.npz> --human-opponent-rate R`. Drawn per game before the --opponents draw (seed-fixed; rate 0 leaves every seat plan unchanged). The human seat samples at temperature 1 from the model's policy over legal moves; only the searching seat is recorded; shards get a per-game `human_opponent` flag. Smoke test (round 66 best vs models/human/net.npz, 50 sims, 4 games, 2 human): the main net won both human games (100 and 155 turns).
  - **Wired 0b32890 (Oct 17 ~19:25Z), off:** loop.py `--human-opponent/--human-opponent-rate`, distributed.py forwards both and rsyncs the model to each host as nets/human_opponent.npz, train.sh passes them only when `HUMAN_OPPONENT_RATE` (workers.env) is nonzero; `HUMAN_OPPONENT` defaults to models/human/net.npz. Turn on (after E14 closes at round 72): set `HUMAN_OPPONENT_RATE=0.15` in workers.env + safe_restart_training.sh. Revert: unset it + restart. Open: which model (models/human/net.npz in use, or models/human-exp best), the rate (start ~10-20%), and the long human-seat games (watch turns per game).
  - **Model choice settled (training worker, Oct 17 20:30Z):** `opponent_model score data/human/holdout/opponent --net round_066 --model <m>`, 16,448 moves (SE ±0.37 pp): models/human/net.npz **66.5% / CE 0.915**; human-exp A 66.9/0.910, B 66.6/0.921, C 66.9/0.908, D 66.5/0.912, E 66.8/0.914. All within ~1 SE; no variant is worth switching to. Keep the default models/human/net.npz.
  - **Probe: what the human seat looks like (training worker, Oct 17 21:55Z)**, local, `selfplay runs/g1/nets/round_066.npz … --human-opponent models/human/net.npz --human-opponent-rate 1`, training sims (400/24, full-rate 0.25), 64 games in 4 shards of 16 (~11 s/game per worker; cheap). Shards in data/orchestrator/scratch/training/e13_probe. Round 66 wins **87.5%** (56/64, ±8) against the human seat; median length 81 turns, same as self-play (no long-game problem). Turn 40+ (4,089 positions): the human seat holds **1.87** unplayed dev cards on average (r69 self-play opponents 1.97), so it does **not** reproduce people's late card holding. The search value is **underconfident** overall (bias −0.138; v>0.5 implies 95.9% vs 97.6% actual) except on the army + dev cards slice, where it is overconfident: bias +0.081, implies 96.0% vs 90.6% (702 positions, few games). Self-play on the same slice is calibrated (+0.003, 93.2 vs 92.1). So the human seat shows a weak hint of the live army+dev overconfidence (live r69 +0.219). At 87.5% wins the seat is a weak opponent; at a rate of 0.15 it gives value targets with clear wins, and the policy targets come from a 400-sim search against a seat it beats easily. Expect E13 to help mainly the value head on human-style positions. Scripts: sp_devs.py DIR TURN, sp_wins.py DIR (win rate is only valid when one seat is recorded, as in human-seat games; shards have no colour column).
  - **Turn 60+ check for live-loss's proposed metric (training worker, Oct 17 21:50Z)**, same 64 probe games, 2,376 positions: the human seat holds **2.09** unplayed dev cards (people live 2.19 [2.06, 2.30], self-play opponents 2.41). The action/VP split of the human seat can't be read from the shards: only the net's seat is recorded, and the opponent's cards are encoded as a count, not by kind. So live-loss's check (opponent's held action cards 0.88 -> 1.37) needs a replay with a per-seat card log, or the bot's own-seat split in games where the human model plays both seats. Army + dev cards slice at turn 60+: bias +0.150, implies 96.9% vs 89.6% actual (516 positions), a clearer hint of the live overconfidence (r69 live +0.219) than at turn 40+.
  - **ARMED (training worker, Oct 18, 12:45 AM Toronto)** after E14 closed: `HUMAN_OPPONENT_RATE=0.15` in scripts/vm/workers.env (model: default models/human/net.npz), HUMAN_POLICY_REPEAT=3 kept, plus safe_restart_training.sh. The restart fires at round 73's promotion match, so round 74 is the first E13 round (its early self-play may mix games from before the restart).
  - **Check (training worker, Oct 18, 1:00 AM Toronto):** the restarted loop runs with `--human-opponent models/human/net.npz --human-opponent-rate 0.15`; all 19 remote hosts plus the coordinator logged "human opponent: 15% of games" for round 74, no errors. But round 74 already had about 4,200 of its 5,000 games from before the restart (67 shards, 4:40-4:54 UTC), so only about 800 games (about 120 against the human seat) are E13. **Round 75 is the first full E13 round**; judge E13 on rounds 75 and later.
  - **Round 73 PROMOTED (training worker, Oct 18, 2:05 AM Toronto), the first new best since round 66 (7 rounds).** Round 73 is an E14 net (no E13 games). Match vs r66 **52.8% ± 2.1% over 2,000 games** (lower bound 50.69%); anchor vs r13 59.7% ± 2.0%; benchmark vs AB:2 96.5%; loop live MSE 0.579 (0.889 / 0.503 / 0.431, 584 games); 69 fresh human games (<100, fresh check skipped). Promoted 06:02:43 UTC. l300 late set (623 games, 18,453 positions) r66 -> r73: all bias +0.186 -> +0.169, MSE 0.412 -> 0.383, paired **-0.017 ± 0.006**; army + dev cards +0.231 -> +0.219, gap 12.9 -> 9.2 pp, paired -0.012 ± 0.010. **E13's baselines are now r73:** metric 3 is the gate vs r73; metric 1 is paired vs r73 as well as r66.

So the seat plays dev cards like the net, not like people, and E13 may not teach the "person sits on knights" pattern. Weighed: it fits the probe above (seat holds 2.09 cards, same total as people, wrong mix). E13 stays on for its 3 rounds (cheap; its value targets still come from human-style board play), but a null E13 would point here. Follow-up if null: a human seat with people's dev-card mix (bar from live-loss: action cards at turn 60+ within [1.29, 1.46] over 48+ games, `scratch/live-loss/seat_devs.py GAMES 60 SIMS`).
  - **VERDICT (training worker, Oct 18, 5:15 AM Toronto): null on the value, no cost to strength.** (1) l300 late set (623 games, 18,453 positions), r76 (2 full E13 rounds) paired vs r73: all -0.001 ± 0.004, army + dev cards **-0.006 ± 0.005**; vs r66: all -0.018 ± 0.008, army + dev -0.018 ± 0.012 (bar -0.10; almost all of that is r73's E14 gain). Army + dev gap 9.1 pp (r73 9.2). (2) Loop live MSE r74/r75/r76: 0.576/0.575/0.576, late 0.428/0.427/0.426 (bar < 0.427): flat. (3) Gate vs r73: r74 49.9%, r75 49.7%, r76 49.5% (round 77 50.4% at 1,000 games): no cost. (4) Rounds still ~47 min; no long-game problem. This fits the live-loss finding: the seat plays its dev cards like the net (0.70 action cards held at turn 60+ against people's 1.37), so it never shows the "person sits on knights" pattern. E13 stays on (free) as the base for the follow-up: a seat that holds its action cards like people (a logit penalty on playing them, calibrated to [1.29, 1.46] held at turn 60+).
  - **Hypothesis:** value targets from games against a human-style seat cut the late overconfidence against people.
  - **Metrics, verdict after rounds 74-76 (3 rounds):** (1) l300 late set paired vs r66: army + dev cards paired ≤ -0.10 is a real late fix; all positions not worse. (2) Loop live MSE late phase below 0.427 (r72) and overall below 0.578. (3) Self-play gate vs best not below ~49% (the human-seat games must not cost strength). (4) Turns per self-play game and round wall time (watch for long human-seat games).
  - **Revert:** delete the HUMAN_OPPONENT_RATE line from workers.env + safe_restart_training.sh.

## E14 — training: learn people's moves from the games they won (owner-approved Oct 16 ~23:30 Toronto, e2d573d)
- **Subsystem:** training. **Status:** ACTIVE (see the status update below). First E14 training round: 69. Set up by Claude Code at the owner's request.
- **Change:** `data/human/train/policy_won` holds the person's moves (decisions while holding no hidden dev card) from the 1,249 training-split games the person won: about 37k moves, z = NaN, so policy-only. `train.py` skips the value loss for those rows. `train.sh` lists the folder `HUMAN_POLICY_REPEAT` times; **3** is about 9% of a round's move targets (about 1.1M full-search self-play targets). `human_dataset.sh` refreshes it before every round.
- **Hypothesis:** the bot's road play improves toward what beats it. People who beat it build fewer dead roads (1.2 vs 2.8 per game) and more settlements (3.5 vs 2.8).
- **Turn on:** set `HUMAN_POLICY_REPEAT=3` in `scripts/vm/workers.env`, then `scripts/vm/orchestrator/safe_restart_training.sh` while round 67 is in self-play or training. That restarts at round 67's promotion match, so round 68 trains with it.
- **Metrics / verdict after rounds 68–71:**
  1. Promotion matches and anchor score are no worse than rounds 62–67 (self-play strength kept).
  2. Top-1 accuracy on `data/human/holdout/policy_won` (5,972 moves the training never sees) rises above round 67's. Use `python -m catanatron.colonist.opponent_model score data/human/holdout/policy_won --net <net>`. Train accuracy far above holdout means memorising: drop to 2.
  3. On live games played by E14 nets, compared with rounds 62–67 games: dead roads and roads before the 3rd settlement (`data/human/index.jsonl` bot stats), and the ranked win rate.
  4. Live MSE (guard) not worse.
- **Revert:** `HUMAN_POLICY_REPEAT=0` in workers.env plus `safe_restart_training.sh`. Nets already promoted stay; the next rounds stop seeing the data.
- **Status update (Oct 17):** ACTIVE since the 14:59Z loop restart (pid 867171, policy_won x3). Round 68 had already trained before it, so **round 69 is the first E14 net** and the verdict window is rounds 69–72.
- **Metric 2 baselines (training worker, Oct 17 18:55Z):** the holdout now has 6,799 moves (228 shards; it was refreshed by human_dataset.sh). Uniform 22.9% / CE 2.224. Round 66 (best): **55.0% / CE 1.389**. Round 68 (last net before E14): **55.2% / CE 1.389**. At n=6,799 the standard error on top-1 is about ±0.6 pp, so look for ≥+1.5 pp in round 69+ before calling it a gain.
- **Metric 4 baselines, late live value (training worker, Oct 17 19:20Z)**, `late_value_set eval` at 6599558 (resignation wins fixed), 322 ranked games / 9,506 late positions: r66 bias +0.161 / MSE 0.360, v>0.5 implied 94.2% vs actual 89.9%; r67 +0.151 / 0.352, 94.1% vs 90.7%; r68 **+0.150 / 0.347**, 94.0% vs 91.0% (±0.9). Already drifting down round over round without E14, so an E14 net must be judged against r68, not r66.
- **Round 69, first E14 net (training worker, Oct 17 21:15Z):** trained 20:58–21:13Z on 13 data folders (r67/r68 had 10; the loop args show policy_won x3), 37,286 games, no NaN. Val policy 0.889 / value 0.397 vs r68's 0.835 / 0.392. The policy number isn't comparable: the validation split now includes human moves, which are harder targets. Value is +0.005, within noise. **Metric 2: holdout top-1 61.7% / CE 1.137 vs r68 55.2% / 1.390** (6,939 moves, 235 shards, uniform 23.0%), so +6.5 pp, well past the +1.5 pp bar. **Metric 4 (late value): +0.144 / MSE 0.340** vs r68 +0.150 / 0.347; v>0.5 implies 94.0% against 91.4% actual. Not worse. Metric 1: round 69's promotion match started 21:13Z (after the restart that added `--before-round gcp_start_spot.sh`). Metric 3 needs live games on an E14 net.
- **Where the late bias sits (r68, same set, diagnostic):** by predicted value v: [-1,-0.5) implied 7.0% / actual 2.1% (3,428 positions, 180 games); [-0.5,0) 36.9% / 15.3% (1,125, 110); [0,0.5) 62.1% / 43.4% (963, 98); [0.5,0.8) 83.6% / 70.6% (964, 97); [0.8,1] 97.3% / 97.4% (3,026, 149). The extremes are calibrated; the contested late positions are 13–22 pp too optimistic, so a temperature fix can't help, and a uniform downward shift wouldn't change move choice. It's people closing contested endgames better than self-play predicts: a play problem (gaps 2–3), not a value-scale one. Dice split (live-loss's lead_dice.json, 149 led-late games): luck ≥ 0, v>0.5 implied 94.3% / actual 95.3%; luck < 0, 94.2% / 87.7%. Once the lead is held, what's left is dice. Scripts: data/orchestrator/scratch/training/lv_buckets.py, lv_dice.py.
- **Late value by what the person holds (training worker, Oct 17 21:40Z)**, `late_value_set eval` at ca6df6e, same 322 games: on the slice where the person holds largest army + 1 or more dev cards (n 3,561, v>0.5 n ~1,050, ±2.6 on actual), r66 bias +0.248, implied 91.7% vs actual 74.6% (a 17.1 pp gap); r68 +0.229, 91.4 vs 76.4 (15.0 pp); **r69 +0.219, 91.2 vs 77.1 (14.1 pp)**. When the person holds no dev card (n 377): calibrated in all three nets (implied ~95% vs actual 99.6%). The gap is shrinking a little each round but is far above live-loss's 5 pp target.
- **Self-play comparison (round 69's shards, all 80, search value v vs z):** at turn 60+ (302,540 positions, 4,765 games) the opponent holds **2.41** unplayed dev cards on average (0: 9.7%, 1: 19.0%, 2: 26.7%, 3: 22.6%, 4+: 22.0%). At turn 75+ the mean is 2.79, and the opponent holds 1 or more in 94.6% of positions (live: 96.0%). So self-play players hold **at least as many** cards as people (live mean ~2.0–2.3); the count is not the distribution gap. Self-play is calibrated on the same slice: army + dev cards, turn 60+, v>0.5, implied 93.6% vs actual 92.5% (n 42,638). **What differs is probably the kind of card:** in self-play, 1.61 of the 2.41 held cards are victory points (knight 0.62, YoP 0.08, monopoly 0.08, road building 0.10). Self-play nets play their action cards promptly and keep VP cards. People who hold action cards (knights, road building) late and convert them are rarely seen in training. Script: data/orchestrator/scratch/training/sp_devs.py ROUND_DIR TURN. Next check (live-loss can run it from captures): how many held cards are action cards for people at turn 60+, against self-play's 0.88.

## E15 — live: free roads from Road Building get the build budget (live-loss, landed 1a586c5, Oct 17)
- **Bug:** `bridge.think_budget` treated any PLAY_TURN decision before the roll as ROLL (cap 1 s, 800 sims at 2400). Road Building played before the roll (134 of 191 bot plays, 70%; 268 of 379 free-road placements, last 300 captures) searched its free roads with that budget (766–963 ms, 800 sims seen live), vs ~5 s / 4800 normally.
- **Change:** `state.is_road_building` uses the PLAY_TURN budget (colonist allocates 15 s for it). Only takes effect after a bot restart (`safe_restart_account.sh <account>`).
- **Metric / verdict:** free-road decisions in bridge.log show ~3.7 s and up to 4800 sims; dead roads and roads-before-3rd-settlement per game (index bot stats) not worse over 100+ games. No strength claim until then.
- **Revert:** `git revert 1a586c5` and restart.
- **Verified (live-loss, Oct 17 ~19:00Z)** from data/bot{3,4,5,7}/bridge.log, free-road placements (the two 'road on edge' lines right after 'play road building'), Oct 16 onward, split at each bot's first restart segment that shows the new budget (capture-name times: bot4 05:36, bot5 06:40, bot7 10:55, bot3 11:06 on Oct 17; so bot7 took effect later than the 03:29Z sync suggested). Script data/orchestrator/scratch/live-loss/e15_freeroad.py (`bot4=20261017-053601 …` for the pooled view, no args for per-segment).
  | bot | pre-roll before: n, med ms, med sims | pre-roll after: n, med ms, med sims | in-turn before → after (med ms / sims) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | bot3 | 144, 961, 799 | 38, 4406, 3848 | 5116/3638 (70) → 4552/4799 (8) |
  | bot4 | 122, 1016, 798 | 54, 4413, 4696 | 5108/4108 (62) → 5132/4028 (34) |
  | bot5 | 148, 1019, 639 | 54, 4456, 3462 | 5104/3264 (60) → 5112/3706 (15) |
  | bot7 | 142, 1011, 799 | 32, 4537, 4048 | 5112/3586 (76) → 5135/3630 (18) |
  Pre-roll free roads went from ~1.0 s / ≤800 sims (556 placements, ~720 games) to ~4.4–4.5 s / 3.5–4.7k sims (178 placements, ~250 games), now in line with in-turn free roads. Every post-restart segment on every bot has a pre-roll median of 4.1–4.8 s (individual placements not checked one by one). The budget part of E15 is verified. Still open: dead roads per game not worse over 100+ games per bot (only ~45–90 games each so far).
- **Dead roads interim (live-loss, Oct 17 ~19:40Z)** from data/human/index.jsonl (bot stats, indexed up to 17:58Z), Oct 15 onward, split at the same per-bot restart times (scratch/live-loss/e15_deadroads.py). Bot dead roads per game, mean ± 95%: pooled 2.27 ± 0.12 (n=982) before vs 2.33 ± 0.31 (n=188) after, difference +0.05 ± 0.33; roads before the 3rd settlement 1.75 ± 0.07 vs 1.77 ± 0.14. Per bot, the differences are +0.15 ± 0.81 (bot3, n=30), −0.03 ± 0.60 (bot4, 68), +0.06 ± 0.62 (bot5, 56) and +0.05 ± 0.67 (bot7, 34). No sign of harm; no bot has 100 games yet. Re-run after the next human_dataset rebuild. (This counts all dead roads, not just free roads, so it would only catch a large effect.)
- **Dead roads re-run (live-loss, Oct 17 21:00Z)**, index rebuilt 20:58Z, same script and cuts: pooled 2.27 ± 0.12 (n=982) before vs 2.38 ± 0.27 (n=244) after, difference +0.11 ± 0.30; roads before the 3rd settlement 1.75 ± 0.07 vs 1.77 ± 0.13; win rate 57% vs 56%. Per bot: bot3 +0.11 ± 0.63 (n=43), bot4 −0.00 ± 0.53 (85), bot5 +0.04 ± 0.58 (68), bot7 +0.35 ± 0.66 (48). No harm shown (the CI excludes a rise of more than ~0.4 roads/game); the after window also spans round 66's deploy (~10 am), so it is not a clean A/B. Only bot4 is near 100 games. **E15 closed as verified (budget) / no harm (dead roads).**

## E17 — live: endgame root filter toward a YoP/trade-funded build (live-loss, PLANNED, not deployed)
- **Code:** `bridge --endgame-filter` (7e574b8, default off) applies `endgame_policy.endgame_root_actions` at the root only. Not yet landed as of Oct 17 15:20Z (land queue blocked).
- **Evidence (endgame_set, e81a9fe, captures bot3/4/5/7 Oct, 2464 games, 927 reached a qualifying position):** games where the bot followed the build path every time won 306/421 = 72.7% (Wilson 95% 68.2–76.7); games with ≥1 miss won 337/506 = 66.6% (62.4–70.6). Two-proportion z ≈ 2.0 (p ≈ 0.05). Confounded: games with a miss have more qualifying turns (longer, closer games), so this does not show the miss caused the loss.
- **Corrected (Oct 17 evening, resignation wins counted, 955 games):** pooled 351/435 = 80.7% vs 396/520 = 76.2%; stratified by qualifying-turn count the gap is +15 pp in 1-turn games (77.0% [72.1, 81.3] vs 61.8% [53.9, 69.2], n=318/152) and about +12 pp weighted over strata. Details in data/orchestrator/live-loss/findings.md.
- **Next / metric:** paired simulated match (seats swapped, shared seeds) filter vs no filter at equal sims, ≥2000 games; adopt live only if ≥ +1.5 pp with CI excluding 0 and no time cost; then 1 account for 200 games vs controls.
- **Revert:** drop the flag (default off).
- **Paired match ready (Oct 17 evening, live-loss), waiting for launch:** 9b82aba adds the registry spec `ZE:<net>:<sims>` (Z plus `endgame_root_actions` as root filter; distributed copies ZE nets). Controller data/orchestrator/jobs/e17-match.py: round 66 ZE vs Z, 400 sims each (live is 2400–4800; 400 keeps 2000 games to ~4–6 h on the fleet next to E18; the filter may matter less at live depth, so a positive result gets a 300-game check at 2400), seats swapped, seeds from 6 400 000 000, chunks of 100, resumable, 2 local workers, GPU host excluded. Output e17-games.jsonl, log e17.log. **Launch (from ~/colonist-ai):** `nohup bash data/orchestrator/jobs/e17-run.sh > data/orchestrator/jobs/e17.log 2>&1 &` (writes e17.pid). Live-loss's shell can't start it. Verdict at 2000 games: adopt for a live A/B only if ≥ +1.5 pp with the 95% interval excluding 0 and the filter's ms per move within 5% of Z's. Revert: kill the pid; nothing deployed.
- **Still not launched (Oct 17 19:35Z).** The fleet is full: round 69 self-play and the E18 ladder share the 2-worker hosts. live-loss is holding off so it doesn't slow round 69. A scratch copy that writes its own pid, data/orchestrator/scratch/live-loss/e17_match.py (same settings and output), can be started with `.venv/bin/python data/orchestrator/scratch/live-loss/e17_match.py` (in the background, log to jobs/e17.log). live-loss will start it once self-play or E18 frees the fleet, unless the lead says otherwise.

Wide minus control is +0.0003 policy, and deep minus control is −0.0007, both under the 0.005 bar. Conclusion: the net is data-limited, not capacity-limited, at the current data volume. ## E16 — training: value target blends the game result with the search value (RUNNING since Oct 17 14:33Z, offline only)
- **Subsystem:** training (offline on the PC GPU; production untouched until E14's verdict).
- **Source:** Lc0's q-ratio; Willemsen, Baier & Kaisers 2021 (value targets in off-policy AlphaZero). E11's diagnostic found the value loss is mostly outcome noise (0.62 early, 0.17 late). `train.py --value-mix` already exists (target (1−m)z + m·v, rows that have a search value only); nothing passes it.
- **Design:** from round 67's net, on round 67's training window, 4 epochs, lr 3e-4, seed fixed, arms m = 0, 0.3 and 0.5. Run between the loop's trainings, holding gpu.lock, like E11b's controller.
- **Metric:** live-holdout value MSE (the loop's live scorer, data/human/holdout/value), overall and in the opening; self-play val value MSE against z. Adopt only if the human MSE falls by ≥0.01 at no policy cost. Then: 1 production round with the chosen m, after E14 closes, gated as usual.
- **Revert:** nothing deployed.
- **Precheck (Oct 17 14:10Z, 10 shards of round 66, 91k rows):** shards store `v`; only 27% of rows are full searches (`full`), so the blend touches ~27% of self-play rows and none of the human rows (z only). Effective blend over the whole mix is about 0.27·m (m=0.5 → ~0.13 overall), so a null result may just mean the arm was too weak; so an m=1.0 arm (m10) was added; the run order is m0, m5, m10, m3. On full rows corr(z, v) = 0.66, MSE(v, z) = 0.557 against var(z) = 0.978. Scripts ready (data/orchestrator/jobs/e16-run.sh, e16-arm.sh, bash -n clean); waiting for the lead's go.
- **Launched Oct 17 14:33Z** on the owner's go (decision e16-launch). Controller pid 861778 (data/orchestrator/jobs/e16.pid), log data/orchestrator/jobs/e16.log; arm output in runs/e16/train.log on the PC. Each arm starts only when runs/g1 step is match or benchmark.
- **Training done Oct 17 16:14Z** (all four arms, no GPU collision with the restarted loop; init was round_066.npz per e16-arm.sh). Self-play val policy 0.845 / 0.844 / 0.844 / 0.844 (m0 / m3 / m5 / m10): no policy cost. Val value 0.509 / 0.442 / 0.410 / 0.378, which is NOT comparable (each arm's target differs). The arms' own holdout.txt says "no live positions": data/human/holdout/value isn't on the PC (and uploading it was denied). Scoring here instead: data/orchestrator/scratch/training/e16_score.py → data/orchestrator/e16/results.txt (pid in jobs/e16-score.pid; nets copied to data/orchestrator/e16/<arm>/net.npz). The coordinator is at load ~50 from self-play, so it is slow.

## Diagnostic (Oct 17 14:50Z, training): the value net is overconfident late against people
- Round 66's net on data/human/holdout/value (541 games, 21196 positions; per-position counts, positions within a game are correlated). Script: data/orchestrator/scratch/training/long_games.py (scratch).
- **By current turn (no hindsight):** bias mean(v) − mean(z) is +0.07 at turns 40–75 and **+0.15 at turns 75–90** (MSE 0.52 vs 0.41), +0.09 at 90+. When v ≥ 0.5 the realised z is +0.83 at turns 40–60, +0.80 at 60–75, **+0.67 at 75–90** (v +0.86, i.e. ~93% claimed vs ~83% won), +0.78 at 90+. Losing positions (v < −0.5) are well calibrated at every turn. The middle band (−0.5..0.5) is optimistic by 0.12–0.25.
- **By final length (hindsight, selection-biased):** 90+ games (113, bot won 48.7%): MSE 0.93, mean v +0.16 vs z −0.03; at turn ≥ 60 with v ≥ 0.5 the realised z is only +0.50 (n 888). <60-roll games (97) are won 92%.
- **Reading:** the net learns from self-play that a lead after turn ~75 converts; people come back from it (gap 3, and live-loss's "fewer cities → 22%"). Comparison metric for E14/E16 and later: turn-75+ bias and v ≥ 0.5 calibration on this holdout, not just phase MSE.

## E18 — search depth: what is a doubling of simulations worth? (live-loss, lead/owner request; RUNNING since Oct 17 17:27Z, pid in e18.pid; first 50 games of 2400 vs 1200: 44.0% ± 13.1%)
- (The lead asked for this as "E17"; E17 was already taken by the endgame filter above, so it is E18.)
- **Question:** live PLAY_TURN gets min(5 s, 2×--simulations) = 4800 sims at --simulations 2400, ~3.7 s per 4800 sims (E15's logs), so the bots are sim-capped with time to spare. If more search is worth Elo, the owner resizes the bot VMs and raises --simulations.
- **Hypothesis:** +X pp per doubling, shrinking with depth; 1200→2400 ≥ 55%, 4800→9600 smaller.
- **Design:** round 66's net (live best) against itself, plain Z spec (the promotion match's settings, no setup noise), paired boards with seats swapped, seeds from 6 180 000 000. Rungs 2400 vs 1200, 4800 vs 2400, 9600 vs 4800, 300 games each, 600 if the 95% interval still holds 50%. Fleet minus the GPU host, 4 local processes, chunks of 50 games, shared with the loop (it will slow the loop's self-play and matches somewhat; the 9600 rung is ~36× a 200-sim match per game).
- **Metric:** paired win rate ± 95% margin for the deeper side, ms per move per process, wall s per game.
- **Files:** controller data/orchestrator/jobs/e18-ladder.py (pid in e18.pid, log e18.log, games in e18-games.jsonl, resumable).
- **Revert:** nothing deployed; kill the pid to stop.
- **Launch (from ~/colonist-ai):** `nohup bash data/orchestrator/jobs/e18-run.sh > data/orchestrator/jobs/e18.log 2>&1 &` (writes e18.pid). Live-loss's Bash can't start background jobs (nohup, wrapper and harness background all denied, Oct 17 16:00Z).
- **Live finding, Oct 17 (bridge.log, last 4000 searches with 1500–5999 sims per account):** the premise that the bots are sim-capped is wrong. Searches that reached ≥4700 sims: bot3 1276 (32%), bot4 1451 (36%), bot5 493 (12%), bot7 1151 (29%). Searches stopped by the 5 s cap with fewer sims: 2638 / 2440 / 3453 / 2786 (61–86%). Median sims 4048 / 4198 / 3396 / 3968 at a median 5.1 s. Throughput median 790 / 823 / 661 / 769 sims/s (p5–p95 about 400–1350). The ~3.7 s per 4800 seen in E15's lines was the fast tail. bot5 (VM shared with bot7, plus E2's opponent model) is the slowest: about 15% fewer sims than bot7 on the same VM. So more CPU per account raises live sims even at the same --simulations. The concurrent-thinking check (sims/s when both accounts on a VM think at once) needs timestamps the logs don't have, or ssh to the VMs; not done.

- **Oct 18, 9:30 AM: why we lose (round 66, 45 completed games: 16 losses, 29 wins; scratch/openhex/losses.py).** Against hexn-4d.0 and ar-PXns-a (7 losses, 11 wins) the openings are even: resources produced by turn 20 are 10.9 vs 10.7 in the losses (13.8 vs 12.4 in the wins), and public VP at turn 40 is 3.7 vs 4.0. The gap opens in the middle game: in the losses the opponent plays 6.6 knights to our 3.4, wins Largest Army in all 7 (we hold it in 10 of the 11 wins), makes twice our bank trades (12.0 vs 5.9) and out-produces us 105 vs 73 over the game, though we built our first city earlier (turn 16 vs 36). The other opponents show the same shape (all 16 losses: we hold Largest Army twice, knights 3.9 vs 6.3, production 80 vs 116; all 29 wins: Largest Army 23 times, production 99 vs 78). Seat matters little (first seat 11-4, second 18-12). So the losses are a lost knight race plus an opponent that converts resources faster through the bank, not bad openings and not the clock. ~~One rules gap fits this: OpenHex plays to 15 VP and our net was trained for 10 VP~~ **WRONG (corrected Oct 18, 11 AM): self-play uses COLONIST_1V1 (15 VP, colonist/rules.py) and so does follow.py; the points target is not a gap.** **No cheap fix in the client:** this is the net's middle-game plan, not a protocol problem; the ROLL budget tweak would touch only roll-vs-knight prompts and is not built. Counts are small (7 strong losses); re-read after ~50 round 73 games.
- **Oct 18, 11:00 AM: OpenHex rolls plain random dice, not colonist's balanced dice (56 saved games, 4356 rolls; scratch/openhex/dice.py).** (1) Every one of the 56 games has a roll colonist's balanced dice cannot make (median: the 8th roll), e.g. the same ordered pair twice before a reshuffle. (2) Spread of totals per game (chi-square vs 2d6, df 10): OpenHex mean 9.5, simulated plain dice 9.9, simulated balanced dice 3.4. (3) Most repeats of one ordered pair within 24 rolls: OpenHex 3.86, plain 3.84, balanced 1.96. Pooled totals match 2d6 (7s: 735 vs 726 expected). The site's guide says "balanced dice", but its games are plain dice. **What this does to us:** the follower's dice notice the first impossible roll and switch to plain odds, so the search's chance nodes are right from then on; but the net then sees dice features it never met in training (balanced flag 0, deck counts frozen at the switch), since self-play is always balanced dice. Its sense of what numbers are worth (and how far behind a bad-luck start is) was learned with balanced dice, which keep each total near its expected count; plain dice are much streakier. Not yet shown to cost games: the next check is a match of round 73 under plain vs balanced dice (evaluate --plain-dice) to see whether its strength drops. Possible fixes are training's call (a share of plain-dice self-play) or, client-side, feeding the net balanced-looking dice features while the search keeps plain odds.
- **Oct 18, 11:15 AM: the unfamiliar dice input does not cost measurable strength; no client change.** (1) Sensitivity, 55 OpenHex games replayed, 4951 of our decisions, round 73 net alone: what the client feeds vs a balanced-looking input (a balanced model fed the same rolls that never switches) changes the top move in 6.8% of positions, mean value shift 0.06 (value spread 0.68). The all-zeros input that `evaluate --plain-dice` gives (dice=None) is much further off: 20% of top moves change, mean value shift 0.16; worth knowing before anyone trusts a --plain-dice match. (2) Strength, 1000 games on plain dice, round 73 vs itself, policy only (no search, where the input matters most): the balanced-looking input scored 51.2% ± 3.1% (95%) against the input the client feeds. So the plain-dice input costs under about 3% even with no search to correct it; with 2400 simulations it should be less. Not worth a client change. Search games at 32 simulations took 2.5 min each on one core, too slow to repeat with search. Scratch: dice_net.py, dice_match.py (on 1 core via detach.py), dice_sum.py.
- **Oct 18, 11:55 AM: boards; a finding, no change.** All 61 saved OpenHex boards use the official number spiral (the A-R token order 5,2,6,3,8,10,9,12,11,4,8,10,9,4,5,6,3,11, laid from an outer corner, skipping the desert). So on OpenHex a 6 never touches an 8 and two equal numbers never touch. Our self-play shuffles numbers freely: 87% of its boards have a 6 next to an 8 and 90% two equal numbers side by side (20,000 simulated boards). Terrain is shuffled on OpenHex (61 distinct layouts); ports sit on the same 9 edges every game with shuffled resources, as in our template. The net has seen boards like OpenHex's (they are a subset of what self-play makes), but at most 1 board in 8. No client change; whether colonist itself places numbers this way is a question for training/live-loss (colonist/rules.py: "the number layout is not confirmed yet"). Script: data/orchestrator/scratch/openhex/boards.py.
- **Oct 18, 11:55 AM: why we lose; a finding, no change.** 54 finished games (32 won, 22 lost). Opening spots are equal: 21.5 vs 22.0 pips for our two first settlements against theirs, in wins and losses alike. In losses the opponent produces 114 cards to our 79. Of that 35-card gap, dice luck is 6 cards (they +3, we -3 against expectation), the robber is even (both lose about 7-8), and 28 cards come from the buildings they add during the game: they out-build us. In wins the mirror image (+18 expected). Over all 54 games production is even (91 vs 92 cards), so our 59% comes from turning cards into points, not from more cards. The knight race and the bank trades follow production: knights 3.7 vs 5.6 and trades 5.9 vs 12.4 in losses, 4.3 vs 2.7 and 10.2 vs 7.5 in wins; trades per card are about 7-11% for both sides either way. No separate knight or trade gap. Script: data/orchestrator/scratch/openhex/luck.py (with losses.py).

## 2026-10-17 18:50Z decision audit (14:50 Toronto)
- **E16 CLOSED: no adoption.** On the human holdout (545 games, 21356 positions) r66 / m0 / m3 / m5 / m10 score MSE 0.584 / 0.584 / 0.584 / 0.584 / 0.583; paired improvement vs m0: m3 +0.0004 [+0.0001, +0.0007], m5 +0.0006 [+0.0002, +0.0011], m10 +0.0012 [+0.0003, +0.0021]. Real but ~10x under the 0.01 bar; turn-75+ bias +0.135-0.139 and v>=0.5 calibration (v +0.87, z +0.70) unchanged in every arm. Self-play val policy unchanged (0.844-0.845). Conclusion: the search-value target does not fix the human-game value error; the late overconfidence is not a target-noise problem. Nothing deployed; artifacts in data/orchestrator/e16 and runs/e16 on the PC stay.
- **E18 interim:** 2400 vs 1200 sims: 47.0% ± 9.9% over 100 paired games (first seat 53%), 2534 vs 1236 ms per move. Inconclusive; continues to 300 (about 20:15Z), extension to 600 if the interval still holds 50%, then 4800 vs 2400. No deployment decision until rung 2.
- **E14:** round 69 (self-play from 18:10Z) is the first E14-trained round; verdict after rounds 69-72 on the four metrics.
- **Research, rejected: board-symmetry augmentation.** encode.py builds per-corner static features (production, ports) with no absolute coordinates, and graph.py's policy head writes settle/city logits from node features, road logits from edge features and robber logits from tile features (graph.py:224-232), so the net is equivariant to the board's 12 symmetries by construction. Rotated or reflected copies would be identical training signal. Data volume (more games per round) remains the lever; E11 showed the net is data-limited.
- Live: 273-198 over 24 h across bot3/4/5/7 (58%); Sherie 2312 (#92). No live experiment running.

## 2026-10-17 9 pm decision audit (Toronto)
- **E17 CLOSED: no adoption.** 47.5% ± 3.2% for the filter over 800 games (upper bound 50.7%, under the 51.5% bar), no time cost. Flag stays default off. Controller pid 896159 still alive and idle (the lead's kill is denied); owner asked to kill it.
- **E18 rung 1 at 500 games (lead's reading):** 2400 vs 1200 sims 51.4% ± 4.2% (first seat 53.0%; 4039 vs 1944 ms per move). A doubling of root search is worth about +1 pp (at most +5.6) in self-play. Consistent with Danihelka et al. 2022 (Gumbel AlphaZero: strength comes mostly from the policy; extra root samples add little once it is good) and with E11 (data-limited net). **Conclusion: bot-VM resizing for more simulations is not justified.** The controller will extend rung 1 to 600 and then start rung 2 (4800 vs 2400, ~8 h of fleet) on its own; the owner is asked to kill pid 879891 (decision kill-e17-e18). Nothing deployed.
- **E20 first live games (Oct 17, 8:19-8:30 PM):** 3 games vs rehex-2.11, 0 wins. Game 1 (9764cd9c) and game 2 (c26d36be): OutOfSync 'unknown event type setupSettlementPlaced' -> fallback moves -> aborted (counts as a loss). Game 3 (1447676d): in sync to log entry 72, then 'MOVE_ROBBER [-1,1,0] isn't legal in the simulator', then 'no fallback move was accepted (pending {})' once per second for 25 s until the worker killed it. Client killed and being fixed; the three raw game files are in data/openhex/games/. Rule: no more queueing until those three logs replay cleanly offline.
- **Value calibration, self-play vs live (job calib-selfplay-r66, round 66 on 79 of its own self-play games, 722,847 positions):** MSE 0.477 overall, opening 0.890, middle 0.491, late 0.339; accuracy 82.2%. Live (round 69's net, 564 games): 0.581 / 0.891 / 0.506 / 0.434. **The opening error is identical (0.89 both): it is not a live gap, just an undecided game.** The whole live gap is late (+0.095) and middle (+0.015). The training brief's gap 1 is rewritten; E13 (human-like opponents in self-play) and the late-lead play are the levers, not the opening.

## E21 — live: an E14 net pinned on one account, bypassing the self-play gate (PLANNED Oct 17 9 pm; code to training, deploy by the lead)
- **Subsystem:** search/live play (bot7 only). No other live experiment is running.
- **Why:** rounds 69 and 70 train on people's moves x3 (E14). Round 69 is equal to round 66 in self-play (50.3% ± 1.5% over 4,000) so the gate keeps round 66, but it predicts human moves 6.5 pp better (holdout top-1 61.7% vs 55.2%, no memorising) and its late live bias is lower (+0.144 vs +0.150). The only metric that matters is live Elo, and the gate will never show it. The owner gave full autonomy on live play; one account first.
- **Design:** a per-account pin (PIN_ROUND=69 in accounts/bot7.env, passed as --pin-round to ranked_runner, empty = follow best_round as now). bot7 (weakest: 22-28 last 50, 2111 Elo) plays round 69; bot3/4/5 stay on best (round 66) as same-hour controls.
- **Metric:** bot7's ranked win rate over its next 200 games (±7 pp) against (a) bot7's previous 200 and (b) bot3/4/5 over the same hours; live-loss's lr_by_round.py on bot7 at 100 games (E14 metric 3); rating trend. Keep/extend to a second account only if bot7 gains ≥ 5 pp relative to the controls' change; revert at once if it drops 10 pp below.
- **Revert:** remove PIN_ROUND from bot7.env on the bot VM and safe_restart_account.sh bot7.
- **Status:** code not yet written (training); deploy at the 9 am run; the pinned sha for round 69 starts 63d05e7d.
- **Oct 17, 9:26 PM (training):** code landed (72389fe). Deploy steps: sync the bot VM (the casual@ service file changed: it passes --pin-round=${PIN_ROUND}, PIN_ROUND defaults to empty), daemon-reload, add PIN_ROUND=69 to accounts/bot7.env, safe restart bot7. Its log then says "using pinned training round 69"; its games.jsonl rows carry "round": 69 and "pinned": true. The pin holds across promotions. bot3/4/5 need no env change (empty = follow promotions as before), but they also pick up the new service file at their next restart. Same landing: the bridge plays the net's move if a search finds no choice at its root (the redealt-15-VP crash).
- **Oct 18, 9:05 AM (live-loss): closed without deploying (lead); E14 live note instead, round 73 (an E14 net) vs round 66 on bot3/4/5/7, ranked.** r66 was live Oct 17, 6:48 AM to Oct 18, 2:16 AM (about 19 h, so "all" = "last 24 h"): 216-149, 59.2% [54.1, 64.1] (365). r73, Oct 18, 2:16 AM to 8:37 AM: 75-47, 61.5% [52.6, 69.6] (122). Per account r66 → r73: bot3 60.0 → 62.5 (85 → 24), bot4 62.9 → 62.9 (97 → 35), bot5 60.9 → 68.6 (87 → 35), bot7 53.1 → 50.0 (96 → 28). Longest road (lr_by_round.py 66, human index): bot takes it first 57.3% [52.1, 62.4] (354) → 59.8% [50.3, 68.6] (107); win when the person takes it first 48.3% [39.6, 57.2] (120) → 34.4% [20.4, 51.7] (32). Dead roads per game 2.29 ± 0.21 → 2.06 ± 0.34; roads before the 3rd settlement 1.79 ± 0.10 → 1.78 ± 0.20. All inside the noise. r73's games so far are all night/morning hours; r66's mostly daytime, so hour of day is a confound until r73 has a full day. ## E22 — efficiency: capacity change, 3 self-play workers in the owner's trial project (CLOSED Oct 20, 11:25 PM Toronto: kept; credit decision due Oct 23)
- **What:** the owner's second GCP project **project-5b3deb12-80e9-4df1-90b** (free trial, ~US$300 credit), reached only through `gcloud --configuration trial2`. Kept strictly apart from colonist-ai-selfplay: own default network, public Ubuntu image, no shared anything; the coordinator reaches the VMs by **external IP** over SSH (the trial's default-allow-ssh rule). The SSH key is the coordinator's worker public key in instance metadata.
- **VMs:** trial-selfplay-1 (us-east1-b), trial-selfplay-2 (us-east1-c), trial-selfplay-3 (us-east1-d), t2d-standard-4, STANDARD provisioning (the trial has no spot), ubuntu-2404-lts-amd64, 20 GB disk, same startup script and sync as the spot fleet (scratch/efficiency/provision_trial.py). About **US$0.17/h each** (VM + disk + external IP), ~US$12/day for the three, from the trial credit, not the main project's US$15/day cap. Uses 12 of the trial's 12 vCPUs.
- **Hypothesis:** each adds what a spot t2d adds (~11-12 s/game per process with 4 procs, ~0.35 games/s), so 3 more hosts at capacity 33 cut a 5000-game round by roughly 15%.
- **Metric:** s/game and shards finished in the next round's remote-<ip>.log; round self-play wall time.
- **Suspension:** if the project stops answering, remove its three hosts from workers.env (both the HOSTS and CAPACITIES lines); the loop already replays an unreachable worker's shards elsewhere.
- **Remove:** take the hosts out of workers.env, then `gcloud --configuration trial2 compute instances delete trial-selfplay-1 --zone us-east1-b` (and -2 us-east1-c, -3 us-east1-d).
- **Oct 17, 10:50 PM (efficiency): 6 more, after the owner's quota raise (CPUS_ALL_REGIONS 48; T2D us-east1 stays 24).** Same recipe (provision_trial.py, all six venv-ready and synced): trial-selfplay-4 us-east1-b 34.23.222.238, -5 us-east1-c 34.26.193.85, -6 us-east1-d 35.231.252.76 (T2D us-east1 now 24/24), -7 us-central1-a 34.59.10.248, -8 us-central1-b 35.223.45.100, -9 us-central1-c 136.80.101.253. Each ~US$0.17/h. Added to both lines of workers.env at :4 procs, capacity 33. Round 72's self-play had not started (still in hooks) and the host list is read at self-play start, so round 72 should be the first with all nine. Ceiling 36 vCPUs (9 VMs) until the owner says "48" (then 3 more in us-central1: 12 VMs ~US$49/day, credit gone in ~6 days).
- **Remove the six:** take them out of workers.env, then `gcloud compute instances delete trial-selfplay-4 --configuration trial2 --zone us-east1-b` (-5 us-east1-c, -6 us-east1-d, -7 us-central1-a, -8 us-central1-b, -9 us-central1-c). Note: `gcloud --configuration trial2 compute ...` is denied by the worker shell; put `--configuration trial2` after the subcommand.
- **Running cost (from VM-hours, ~US$0.17/VM-h):** 1-3 since 9:20 PM Oct 17, 4-9 since 10:50 PM Oct 17. Burn now 9 × 0.17 = **US$1.53/h, ~US$37/day**. Spent to Oct 17, 10:50 PM: ~4.5 VM-h ≈ US$0.80. With ~US$300 credit the nine last ~8 days: credit runs out around **Oct 25**. Raise a decision for the owner by Oct 23.
- **Oct 17, 11:45 PM:** spent so far ~12.8 VM-h ≈ US$2.20; burn still US$1.53/h. Round 72 (first with all nine): trial-4..9, outside the round-71 match's host list, play **7.7-9.8 s/game** per process; trial-1..3 and the spot VMs, shared with that match, 15.5-18 s.
- **Oct 20, 11:25 PM (efficiency): closed, kept.** All nine trial VMs are RUNNING and in workers.env; they are now a standing part of the fleet. Still open: about 3.1 days at ~US$37/day ≈ US$115 spent, so the credit ends around Oct 25-26; raise the owner's decision by Oct 23.
- **Oct 21, 9:20 AM (efficiency): brief for the owner (decision e22-trial-credit).** (scratch/efficiency/trial_share.py over rounds 165-185's 'self-play games by host'.) The nine play **41-46% of each round's games** (2,500-2,700 of 6,000), about 3.6 games/s together. Without them the rest of the fleet plays about 2.1 games/s: a 6,000-game round's self-play would take about 22 min instead of 12.5, longer than training's window (self-play + idle is about 15.5 min), so rounds would slow or shrink to about 3,400 games. The project also holds **gpu-train-1** (n1-standard-8 spot + 1 T4, 100 GB pd-balanced, created Oct 18 6:15 PM, last start Oct 19 9:28 AM), which is **COLONIST_TRAIN_HOST**: training runs on it. Estimated cost (list prices; gcloud billing is denied): nine VMs ~US$1.53/h, GPU host ~US$0.23/h, together ~US$1.76/h ≈ **US$42/day**. Spent to Oct 21 9:15 AM: ~746 VM-h × 0.17 ≈ US$127 + GPU ~US$13 ≈ **US$140 of ~US$300**; ~US$160 left ≈ 3.8 days: the credit ends **about Oct 25 morning**, and the GPU host stops with it. Options: (a, recommended) keep all nine until ~US$40 is left (about Oct 24 midday), then delete them; the GPU host alone (~US$5.5/day) runs about 7 more days; training goes back to 5,000 or fewer games per round. (b) Keep three now (~US$18/day with the GPU host, ~9 days), losing about 2/3 of the trial's games today. (c) Delete all nine now; the GPU host lasts ~29 days. Owner to check: the billing page for the real balance, and whether the trial account is upgraded to paid (then spending past the credit is charged to the card instead of the VMs stopping).

At that time (efficiency's first run after 6 AM Oct 24): take the nine out of workers.env (HOSTS and CAPACITIES lines) between rounds, then `gcloud compute instances delete trial-selfplay-N --configuration trial2 --zone Z` for all nine (zones above), and confirm with `gcloud compute instances list --configuration trial2` that only gpu-train-1 is left. ## E26 — efficiency: shorter self-play tail (two scheduling fixes; DEPLOYING Oct 18, 4:20 AM Toronto)
- **Why:** round 77's self-play took 17.5 min (07:44-08:01 UTC), r76 20.5, r75 19. (a) The 4-process VMs get 5 shards: 4 run together (~490 s), then the 5th runs alone on one process (~500 s more) with three idle. (b) Rounds 75 and 76 then waited on one 2-core AWS worker's single 64-game shard (30 s/game per process, ~16 min); idle hosts could only take *unstarted* shards.
- **What:** (a) 1cff15c: the shards past the last full wave are cut into parts for every process (byte-identical merge, tested). Remotes get it at each round's code sync: live from round 78. (b) 378f6fe: when nothing unstarted is left, an idle host with over twice a running host's capacity also plays that host's last missing shard; the first copy is kept and the other host is stopped. Coordinator-side, so it needs the training restart: safe_restart_training.sh, run Oct 18, 4:20 AM (restarts at the next promotion match).
- **Fleet rate check (rounds 75-77 logs):** big VMs 7.1-8.0 s/game per process (~0.53 games/s each); 2-core hosts 20-30 s. The capacity weights are all ~0.62x the measured rate, so their ratios are right; no recalibration.
- **Metric:** self-play wall time ('self-play by' → 'self-play complete' in prefetch.log), expect ~11-12 min vs 17.5-20.5; 'in N parts' and 'also plays' lines. Verdict after rounds 78-80.
- **Play unchanged:** same seeds and games; a duplicated shard is the same bytes.
- **Revert:** git revert 378f6fe (and/or 1cff15c), land, safe_restart_training.sh.
- **Oct 18, 7:50 AM, round 80 (first round uninterrupted with both fixes):** 7:29:48 to 7:47:14 AM, 17.4 min, of which 3.3 min set-up (E28 not yet live) and **14.1 min of play** (r75-77: about 16-17 min of play). Better, a little short of the 12-13 min hoped for. Verdict: keep. Self-play is off the critical path anyway (it runs alongside training and the match).

## E29 — efficiency: split the promotion match by measured host speed (DEPLOYING Oct 18, 7:55 AM Toronto)
- **Why:** the match is what sets rounds per hour (self-play runs alongside it). Its games were split by process count, but per-process speed differs up to 8x: the coordinator (8 processes, capacity 8) got 82 of every 1000 games, a t2d VM (4 processes, capacity 33) got 41. So the fast VMs idled while the coordinator and the 2-core hosts finished. Round 78: 13.5-16 min per 1000 games.
- **What:** 37a5f53: run_tasks uses COLONIST_SELFPLAY_CAPACITIES (the self-play weights) and logs 'evaluation games by host: ...' with each host's games and seconds.
- **Metric:** minutes per 1000 match games in train.log (r78: 15.8 and 13.6). Expect about 4-6 min. The new log line shows the slowest host.
- **Play unchanged:** a game is a pure function of its task; only where it is played changes (tested: same results as local play).
- **Deploy:** the restart queued for E28 fired at 7:47:50 AM, a minute before the landing reached ~/colonist-ai, so a second restart at 7:50 AM (round 79's match had just begun, round 80's self-play was done; cost about 2 min of match games).
- **Oct 18, 8:00 AM, first reading (round 79's first 1000 games):** 7:50:07 to 7:56:56 AM, **6.8 min per 1000 games (r78: 15.8 and 13.6), about 2.1x faster**. By host: coordinator 14 games in 119 s, GPU host 33 in 149 s, the 16 t2d VMs 57-58 each in 287-372 s, the slow hosts 6-22 in 210-410 s (slowest 3.17.59.49, 410 s). Those slow hosts also ran the lead's E18 ladder games at the same time. The split is now even; little tail left. Second reading: the next 1000.
- **Oct 18, 8:35 AM, verdict: works, keep.** Round 79's whole 4000-game match ran 7:50:07 to 8:16:32 AM: **26.4 min, 6.6 min per 1000 games** (r78: 13.6-15.8), about 2.2x faster. Second 1000: slowest host 401 s, median about 315 s. Last 2000: slowest 774 s (3.17.59.49), median about 620 s, coordinator 167 s, GPU host 278 s; an even split would take about 610 s. That leftover tail is E30.
- **Revert:** git revert 37a5f53, land, safe_restart_training.sh.

## E30 — efficiency: later match calls split by each host's measured match speed (CLOSED Oct 18, 9:30 AM Toronto: works, kept; verdict under E32)
- **Why:** self-play speed is a rough guide to match speed. The GPU host trains during self-play, so its self-play speed is low; in round 79's match it finished in 145-278 s against a median of 315-620 s. The 2-core hosts were 25% slower than the median. Summing each host's measured rate, the last 2000-game call could have taken about 610 s, not 774.
- **What:** run_tasks records each host's games per second (only hosts that returned all their games). The next call splits by those speeds; a host with no speed yet gets its self-play capacity times the median measured ratio. The first call after a restart still splits by self-play speed.
- **Metric:** minutes per 1000 match games (r79: 6.6); the gap between the slowest host and the median in 'evaluation games by host' lines, calls 2+ of a match. Expect about 5.5 min per 1000. Verdict after two matches.
- **Play unchanged:** a game is a pure function of its task; only where it is played changes.
- **Deploy:** land, then safe_restart_training.sh (coordinator code).
- **Revert:** git revert the commit, land, safe_restart_training.sh.

## E25 — efficiency: the coordinator plays its self-play range as a job, and a smaller share for it (CLOSED Oct 20, 11:25 PM Toronto: works, kept)
- **Why:** round 72's self-play took 37 min (03:04-03:41 UTC; r71 65 min). ## E24 — efficiency: deploy two loop speedups (5175570, 7697c46) by a safe training restart (SCHEDULED Oct 17, 10:10 PM Toronto)
- **What:** (1) a round whose early self-play already played every shard skips its pre-round hooks and the self-play step (round 70 lost 19 min there, round 71 again sat in hooks after its match at 10:02 PM); (2) shards of a host that could not be prepared or failed are taken by idle machines during the wave, not after the slowest host (round 71: about 9 min).
- **How:** safe_restart_training.sh, which restarts colonist-train when round 71's promotion match begins. The loop resumes the match; the round 72 prefetch that had just started is relaunched (loses seconds of games). Workers get the code from sync_workers.sh at service start.
- **Metric:** gap between a round's verdict and 'training on' (19 min in r70 → under 1 min when the prefetch finished); 'takes ... from <host>' lines and round self-play wall time (r71: 65 min). Verdict after 2 rounds.
- **Play unchanged:** scheduling only; moves and games are the same code.
- **Revert:** git revert the two commits, land, safe_restart_training.sh.
- **Oct 17, 11:47 PM (training), round 72, first reading:** round 71's verdict at 11:46:34 PM, "round 72: training on" at 11:46:42 PM: **8 s gap** (r70: 19 min). Round 72's self-play had finished during the match ("played early"), so the hooks and self-play step were skipped as designed. Round 73 self-play started at the same moment. Second round to judge: round 73.
- **Oct 18, 12:45 AM (training), second reading, verdict: works.** Round 72's verdict at 12:40:00 AM, "round 73: training on" at 12:40:08 AM: **8 s gap** again; round 73's self-play had finished during round 72's match, and round 74's started at once.

## E27 — training: the human seat (E13) holds its knights like people (STARTED Oct 18, 6:55 AM Toronto)
- **Why:** gap 1, and E13's null verdict. The human seat played its dev cards like the net (0.70 action cards held at turn 60+ against people's 1.37), so self-play never showed a person sitting on knights, the live army + dev-card slice where the net is most overconfident (+0.22 bias).
- **Change:** the seat's model file carries meta_dev_play_bias = -3 (code 168c141), added to the logits of playing an action card. Calibration (training worker, r66 net vs the seat, 100 sims, 48 games per bias, scratch/training/holddev.py): bias 0: 0.70 [0.50, 0.94] action cards held at turn 60+ (live-loss, 16 games); -1: 0.92 [0.63, 1.27]; -2: 1.04 [0.70, 1.42]; **-3: 1.56 [1.11, 2.11]** (people 1.37 [1.29, 1.46]; the interval covers people's). The net still wins 45/48 against it; median length 77 turns. Deployed: `HUMAN_OPPONENT=/home/owen/colonist-ai/data/orchestrator/training/human_holddev_-3.0.npz` in scripts/vm/workers.env (rate stays 0.15), safe_restart_training.sh.
- **Hypothesis:** value targets from games where the opponent sits on knights teach the net people's late comebacks, lowering its late overconfidence on the army + dev slice.
- **Metrics / verdict after 3 full rounds:** (1) l300 late set (late_value_set eval, data/orchestrator/live-loss/late_value_set_l300.jsonl), army + dev paired vs r73 ≤ -0.10 for adoption, ≤ -0.03 counts as a real signal; (2) loop live MSE late < 0.426; (3) gate vs best ≥ ~49% (no strength cost). First full round: the one after the restart's round.
- **Revert:** remove the HUMAN_OPPONENT line from scripts/vm/workers.env (back to models/human/net.npz) and run safe_restart_training.sh.
- **Oct 18, 7:30 AM:** live since the 7:00 AM restart (loop args show human_holddev_-3.0.npz). Round 79's self-play (5,000 games, all after the restart) is the first E27 round. In its 716 human-seat games the seat held 2.19 dev cards on average at turn 60+ (all kinds, seen from the net seat), against 1.99 in round 78's 735 (old seat) and 2.38 in plain self-play: up about 0.2, so the bias works but moves the count less than the calibration's action-card figure. Net wins 78.4% vs 77.7%; search value bias in those games -0.12 vs -0.11. Script scratch/training/e27_check.py ROUND_DIR. Full rounds: 79, 80, 81.
- **Oct 18, 9:05 AM (training): verdict NO EFFECT, kept on (harmless).** l300 late set, army + dev paired vs r73: r79 -0.019 (±0.010), r80 -0.019 (±0.011), against r77 (old seat) -0.011 (±0.006): under the -0.03 signal bar. All positions: r79 -0.002, r80 +0.001. Loop live MSE late: r79 0.423, r80 0.423 (r77 0.424, r78 0.420). Gate vs r73: r78 49.7%, r79 50.3%, r80 51.0% (no strength cost). Round 81 (training now) to be added, but two rounds at -0.019 can't reach -0.10. The seat stays on: costs nothing, and E31 measures from the same baseline. Revert as above if wanted.
