## E81 — training: the people model replaces AB:2 in the promotion gate (owner's question Oct 21, 1:48 PM; lead's design brief 1:55 PM Toronto; PROPOSED, not live, waits for the owner's go)
- **What:** the gate's third check, today 400 games against AlphaBeta depth 2 (AB:2, must not drop 3 points below the best's), becomes a match against a fixed copy of the people model. Same shape as the round 13 anchor: a net that passed its promotion match plays it, both sides net plus 200-simulation search (the anchor's search), 4,000 games on fixed boards (seed 3,600,000,000), and is kept as best's challenger only if it scores no more than 2 points below the best's score. The best's score is the one it got on its own promotion, so the reference moves with each promotion. The round 13 anchor stays.
- **Why:** AB:2 is saturated: 94.2-97.2% for every promoted net since round 63 (r63 96.2, r66 94.2, r73 96.5, r150 97.2, r152 96.2, r168 95.2, r182 95.5, r189 96.5; ± 1.8 at 400 games), and it has held a net back once, at round 1. The anchor still moves (r73 59.7%, r152 62.3%, r168 64.2%, r182 64.9%, r189 64.6%).
- **Opponent:** E68's snapshot of the people model (fit from round 152, sha 84f516847a618134), copied to data/orchestrator/e81/people_net.npz. Not models/human/net.npz: it is re-fit every hour or so starting from the current best (refresh.json "init": runs/g1/best.npz), so it would move under the gate and become the candidate's own family.
- **Game count:** 4,000. One score is ± 1.55 points; the check compares two scores (candidate and best), so the gap is about ± 2.2 (less, as they share boards). A net exactly as strong as the best is then held back about 1 time in 30 by chance; at 2,000 games about 1 in 10 (the anchor's rate today).
- **Cost:** 4,000 games take about 18 min of fleet (the anchor's 2,000 took 535 s at round 189), only on rounds where a net passed its match (2 of the last 22 rounds: 182, 189). AB:2's 103 s goes away. The first candidate after deploy also scores the best once (another 18 min) unless state.json already holds the best's people score.
- **Code (landed 5539d99, off by default):** loop.py `--people PATH --people-games 4000 --guard-people 0.02`. Deploy: in scripts/vm/train.sh add `--people data/orchestrator/e81/people_net.npz` and `--benchmark ""`, then safe_restart_training.sh. Dropping AB:2 also drops its diagnostics file and the site's benchmark column; keeping AB:2 as a reading only (`--guard-benchmark 1`) costs the same 103 s.
- **Revert:** remove both flags from train.sh and safe_restart_training.sh.
- **Reference (Oct 21, 2:42 PM): round 189 55.40% ± 1.5** (2216-1784 over 4,000 games, seed 3,600,000,000, first seat 50.8%, 3,123 s at niceness 19 beside round 190's gate). Round 182's arm (same seed) follows (scratch/training/e81_ref.py, data/orchestrator/e81/results.txt). **Across promotions it barely moves:** round 73 54.1% ± 2.2, round 152 56.0% ± 2.2 (E68, 2,000 games, another seed), round 189 55.4% ± 1.5, while the anchor rose 59.7% -> 64.6% over the same nets. So the check is flat, not a ladder: it would catch a net that drops 2+ points against people-like play, but it will rarely block, and a block would mean a real change in style. AB:2 also never blocks, so the swap adds a check that can block where none can today.
- **DEPLOYED to main (training, Oct 21, 2:52 PM Toronto; landed cea818b):** train.sh passes `--people data/orchestrator/e81/people_net.npz --people-games 4000 --guard-people 0.02 --guard-benchmark 1` unless PEOPLE_GATE is set empty in workers.env. AB:2 keeps playing on candidate and promoted rounds as a reading (log line, diagnostics, site column) but can't block. That stands in for the lead's "once a day" AB:2 line; it plays whenever the net changes, which is when a new reading means anything. safe_restart_training.sh requested 2:52 PM; it fires when round 191's match starts (about 3:00 PM). Round 190 kept on the old rule (50.22% over 4,000). state.json has no people score yet, so the first net that passes its match triggers one 4,000-game scoring of round 189 by the loop at normal priority (about 18 min), then the candidate's own 4,000. Revert: `PEOPLE_GATE=` in scripts/vm/workers.env, then safe_restart_training.sh.
- **Caveat:** the snapshot is fit from round 152, so it may judge round 152's family kindly (E68's caveat). E68 also showed it does not see the live gap between rounds 73 and 152; it is a check against a slide in play against people-like moves, not a live predictor.
- **APPROVED by the owner (Oct 21, 1:54 PM Toronto); DEPLOYING (lead's note 2:45 PM).** Order given to training 1:58 PM: reference first (round 189, 4,000 games, running since 1:50 PM at low priority), then the loop change on main (AB:2 out, people model in, drop of at most 0.02 vs the best's reference), `safe_restart_training.sh` between matches (round 190's gate on the old rule if the reference is late, switch at round 191). AB:2 stays as a plain daily log line off the gate for a week. Training reports the restart time, the reference score and the first gated round by 9 PM.
- **FIRST NET THROUGH THE PEOPLE CHECK (training, Oct 22, 4:57 AM Toronto): round 208 PROMOTED, the first new best since round 189 (19 rounds).** Match 51.41% ± 1.03 over 8,000 (near miss 51.2% at 4,000, then 4,113-3,887). People check: the loop's own scoring of round 189 55.4% ± 1.5 (2,216-1,784, identical to the offline reference, same seed), round 208 **57.4% ± 1.5** (2,297-1,703), so +2.0 over the best; the check held with room (it blocks at -2). Anchor vs round 13: 66.8% ± 2.0 (round 189 64.6%). AB:2 reading 96.2% (385-15). Live value MSE at round 208: 0.556 (0.874 / 0.482 / 0.386), flat. Cost of the people check this round: 2 × about 16 min (961 s, 964 s) of fleet.

## E82 — research: does the net see a thin Longest Road lead slipping away? (lead's research item, Oct 21, 2:45 PM Toronto; ORDERED, study, offline, no fleet time)
- **Evidence (live-loss, Oct 21, 2:20 and 2:30 PM; 1,647 games vs 2100+ players):** in games of 70+ rolls the bot holds Longest Road at roll 60 in 48% of games (people 29%) but keeps a 1-road lead 32.0% [24.6, 40.5] of the time and a 2-road lead 37.1% [28.2, 47.0]; people keep theirs 91.7% and 87.5%. Margin 3+: 74.7% vs 95.4%. Largest Army shows no gap (79% vs 82%). The chaser adds 4.84 roads to the holder's 3.42. Building the extra road at once does not fix it: road_def.py playouts, 187 positions × 16 games, net vs net −0.4 [−1.9, +1.2], vs the people model +0.9 [−0.7, +2.6]. So the skipped road is not the fault; the question is whether the value sees the threat at all.
- **Hypothesis:** the net prices a margin-1 Longest Road lead like a safe 2 points. In a 15-point game the swing is 4 points of margin, and the race spans several turns, beyond 2,400-simulation search depth. If the value is blind here, more search will not help; a target will.
- **Method (research, offline):** from the holdout captures (data/human/holdout and the 2100+ set live-loss uses), every position at roll 60 where the bot holds the road by 1 or 2 in a 70+ roll game; the best net's stated win chance there against the realized result, split by kept/lost the road, and the same for margin 3+ as the control. Then at the moment the road is lost: the net's value drop versus the realized swing. Tools to reuse: live-loss's long_games.py (the game set and the margins) and late_cal.py (calibration by phase); replay observer notes in live-loss's progress file.
- **Verdict rule:** a calibration gap (stated minus realized) of 10 points or more for margin 1-2 holders that is absent for margin 3+ means blind; propose a training-side fix (an auxiliary target "who holds Longest Road at the end", in the spirit of KataGo's auxiliary ownership targets that sharpen the value head, or E65's road plans). A gap under 5 points means the net sees it and plays elsewhere on purpose: hand it to live-play and live-loss (where the roads go, the chaser's race).
- **Cost:** no money, no fleet. Report by 9 PM or 9 AM.
- **RESULT (research, Oct 21, 3:55 PM Toronto; scratch/research/e82.py, rows in data/orchestrator/e82/rows.json, log jobs/e82.log):** 1,316 live games vs 2100+ with 60+ rolls replayed (all of long_games.py's set that reached roll 60), the best net (round 189) evaluated at each bot roll from roll 40. Stated = (v+1)/2 at the bot's first roll at 60-63; game-bootstrapped 95% ranges. Games of 70+ rolls (1,077):
  - Bot holds by 1: stated 58.8, won 39.7, gap +19.1 [+11.8, +26.0], kept 47.9% (121). By 2: 52.3 / 38.7, +13.6 [+7.2, +21.1], kept 50.5% (93). By 3+: 67.4 / 52.4, +15.0 [+10.6, +20.0], kept 84.5% (252). Thin (1-2) together: +16.7 [+11.6, +22.1] (214); thin minus wide about +1.7 (± about 7).
  - Nobody holds: +14.2 [+9.8, +19.2] (234). Person holds by 1: +18.3 [+11.3, +25.4]; by 2: +8.6 [+1.5, +15.7]; by 3+: +6.0 [+1.6, +10.8]; person thin together +13.8 [+8.9, +19.2] (177), kept 80%.
  - At equal VP gap, thin vs wide for the bot's road mixes both ways (VP +3 or more: +19.1 vs +13.2; VP +2: +16.3 vs +22.3; level: +1.4 vs +19.7; small cells).
  - The bot loses the road after roll 60 (335 games): value drops 13.0 points [11.2, 14.8] (54.2 to 41.2); it then wins 28.1%, so still too sure by +13.2 [+9.8, +16.5]. Margin 1-2 losses (277): drop 12.4, after-gap +12.5; margin 3+ (58): drop 15.7, after-gap +16.6.
  - Note: kept rates here (48-50% for the bot's thin leads) are higher than live-loss's 32-37% because the snapshot is the bot's own roll at 60-63 and "kept" is at the bot's last roll, not the game's end.
- **Verdict (rule above):** NOT BLIND. The gap for thin leads is 10+ points, but the same gap is there for wide leads (+15.0) and with nobody holding (+14.2), so it is the known late overconfidence against people, not a road-race blind spot. The net prices a thin lead 11 points lower than a wide one and drops 13 points when the road goes. A "who holds Longest Road at the end" target is not supported. The road play (where the roads go, the chaser's race) goes to live-play and live-loss. The one road-specific hint, the net too sure when the person holds a thin lead (+13.8 vs +6.0 wide; the net seems to expect to win it back, people keep it 80%), feeds research's late-game target proposal (task 3).

## E61 — RERUN #2 PREPARED on Dilly6283 alone (live-play, Oct 21, 2:50 PM Toronto; NOT started; starts only on the lead's go)
- **Condition (lead, 2:45 PM):** Finlay3621 (bot10) has moved to bot VM 4; E80 closed on Dilly6283 and bot3.env restored to bot3.env.bak-20261021-e80 (standard clock); then a full day of Dilly6283 alone on VM 2 with its search rate confirmed at about 1,088 sims/s (per_game_rate.py bot3 / rate_tail.py from the post-move start line, moves of 4.5 s or more; accept 950+). The lead gives the go at a report.
- **Quit-rate rule for this and every live verdict (from live-loss's finding, Oct 21, 3:27 PM):** report both the raw record and the record over finished games (quit_rate.py, live-loss's scratch), and the quit share for each arm. A gap carried only by more opponent quits is not counted as a gain. Compare finished-game records within the same 100-point rating band, not raw, when the arms' opponents differ in rating (stronger people quit less and win more; live-loss, 3:31 PM: E54's finished gap is -19 raw but -4.2 [-26, +19] band-matched; e54_live.py prints both). Raw is fine when the arms' opponent ratings are close (E80: Dilly6283 vs Finlay3621). **Amended Oct 21, 3:40 PM (live-loss, quit_timing.py over 1,914 games):** people quit around roll 51 with the bot about 3.4 VP ahead; only 24% [20, 28] of quits come with the score within 1 VP. Most quits are decided games, so they count as real wins, and dropping them biases an arm with more quits low (E54: its 51 quits came at 4.2 ± 0.7 VP ahead, 16% early, vs Finlay3621's 3.8 ahead). The finished-games rate is a rating-matched cross-check only, never a harm stop on its own. What to check per arm instead: the quit share, the VP lead at the quit, and the share of early quits (within 1 VP); only an arm whose extra quits are early ones has its gain discounted.
- **Setting (same as the stopped rerun):** add to bot3.env on bot VM 2 `THINK_BUDGETS=PLAY_TURN=0.3:10:5,MOVE_ROBBER=0.4:10:5,ROLL=0.33:2:0.6667` (backup first: bot3.env.bak-<date>-e61b), then safe_restart_account.sh bot3 between games; confirm bridge.log's 'think budgets from THINK_BUDGETS' line shows the three entries and round 73 pinned.
- **Baselines:** (a) round 73, standard clock, alone: 164-100 (62.1%) before Oct 20, 8:55 PM; (b) the day alone before the start (the gate day) as a fresh same-conditions baseline. Control: Finlay3621 on VM 4 (same net, standard clock).
- **Harm stop (as before):** from 75 games, a win rate under 50%, or 7 points under the baseline with the 95% range excluding zero, or any game lost on time: remove the line, safe restart.
- **Verdict:** 300 games, by opponent band, against baseline and colonist's expected score (e61_band.py START bot3,bot10); keep and extend at +3 or more, undo at -3 or worse, between: 600 games. Also games a day (pace.py): the first run cost 25-30% fewer games.
- **Speed check during the run:** 10 s should buy about 10,000-12,000 sims at 1,088/s; if the median move under the 10 s cap falls under 8,000 the test is confounded again: report to the lead.
- **Revert:** restore the backup (or delete the THINK_BUDGETS line), safe_restart_account.sh bot3 between games.

## E83 — research: E54 resumed, step 30 to step 60 (owner's decision Oct 21, 3:23 PM, changes 3:25 and 3:28 PM, via the lead; LIVE since Oct 21, 3:35 PM Toronto; deadline the 9 PM report)
- **What:** E54's policy-gradient loop, picked up from run 1's step 30 (data/orchestrator/e54/run1/iter-30/net.npz) for 30 more steps, with run 1's settings unchanged: 20,000 games a step (half against itself, half against rounds 73, 66, 13 and the people model), 1 epoch of train.py --pg at lr 3e-5, KL 0.02 to round 73, same seed scheme. A holdout error worse than step 30's by 0.01 or more goes to the owner as a flag before anything goes live.

Not the account: on step 30 both before 7:45 PM, Tedi4960 was −5.8 (−23.0 to +11.8) against fymouushii. Within Tedi4960, step 50 vs its own step 30: +11.1 (−3.1 to +25.8). Against colonist's expected score both are level (−2.1 and +0.1), as each account sits at its own rating. Same window since Oct 21, 7:45 PM: step 50 69-37 (65.1% [56, 73], opponents 2124) vs fymouushii step 30 57-60 (48.7%, opponents 2088). Same band **+15.1 [+1.6, +28.4]**, still clear of zero. Against colonist's expected: step 50 -6.4, step 30 -1.0 (each account sits near its own rating). Finished only: 53.2% ± 11.0 vs 33.3% ± 9.7. Quits 25.5% vs 23.1%. Verdict at 300.
- **Research's case for ONE recipe change (lead's task, Oct 21, 7:55 PM; written 8:15 PM Toronto; no run until the owner answers):** **the value head should learn from production's data, not from the policy-gradient games.** Why: the people error is something E54 *does* to the net, not where it started. Round 73, its start, reads 2100+ games at 0.610 (better than round 189's 0.619); step 10 was already 0.635, step 30 0.646, then flat to step 60. In --pg the value head trains on GAE returns from its own games, half of them against rounds 66 and 13 and a people model that lose most of the time, and those returns lean on the value head itself. That teaches "a lead late is a win", which is the late overconfidence we see (late 0.424-0.429 vs round 189's 0.397). Since search leans on the value, this is the most likely reason a net 4 points up on round 189 in self-play plays no better than round 152 against people. The policy side is fine: keep it. **Why not the others:** (a) starting from round 189: round 73 started *better* on people (0.610), so the start isn't the cause; it would also throw away the policy gain and the result could not be compared with E83. (b) KL prior to the current best: the policy was not held still by the KL (validation policy 0.605 → 0.575, KL 0.104 → 0.128 over steps 31-60), yet the raw score did not move, so a looser leash is unlikely to unflatten it. (c) more people-model games, with the current re-fit model: it loses 78-88% of its games to the net, so the reward is almost always "win", which gives little to learn and, if anything, more overconfidence; E13, E27 and E55 were null. **What it changes in the two flat numbers:** the 2100+ error should come back to production's level (0.62); the raw score should stay where it is (about 56%), because raw play does not use the value head. I don't have a change that I expect to move the raw score: it went flat by step 10 (about 200,000 games) under every setting we tried, and I would not spend a run on it. **Design, cheapest first:** step 1, no VM: take step 50 and retrain only its value on production's latest window (the same rows and value mix round 189 trained on, which already include people's games), 1 epoch at lr 3e-5, with a KL of 0.02 to step 50's own policy so the moves stay put. That needs a small train.py option (a value-only mode with the KL prior set to the starting net; about 1-2 h to build and test; old nets load unchanged). Step 2, only if step 1 passes: E54's loop for 10 steps from step 50 with the PG rows' value weight at 0 and production rows mixed in for the value. **Signals and stop rule (step 1):** 2100+ holdout error (243 games, paired) must reach 0.630 or lower, at least half the gap to round 189; the policy must stay put (top-move agreement with step 50 at least 95% on validation); then 2,000 games at 200 simulations against step 50 and 2,000 against round 189. Stop and close the idea if the error does not reach 0.630, or if the net loses to step 50 (under 50% over 2,000 games). Pass: error 0.630 or lower and at least 54% against round 189; then a third live arm is the owner's call. **Cost:** step 1: no money; about 1 h of the PC's GPU (training must be told first; it takes the PC's self-play share for that hour) and about 35 min of fleet time for the 4,000 games. Step 2: one 16-vCPU spot VM for about 1.5 h (about US$0.30) and about 1.5 h of the PC's GPU. **Verdict in:** step 1 in one evening; step 2 about 2 h after that.

## E84 — research: the late overconfidence is about people's positions, not people's late play (research task 3, Oct 21, 4:15 PM Toronto; STUDY done, step 1 FAILED Oct 22, 7:50 AM)
- **Question:** the net is too sure late against people (E82: +14-17 points at roll 60 vs 2100+). Is that because people out-play it late, or because people reach positions the net misjudges whatever the play?
- **Method (no fleet, no GPU; scratch/research/e84_cal.py and e84_sp.py):** E71's 2,085 holdout starts (turns 50-90 of people's games). Round 73's raw stated win chance (bot's chair) at each start against three outcomes from the same start: the live game, round 73 vs itself (E71 arm), round 73 vs the people model (E71 arm). Control: round 189 on round 190's own self-play rows at turns 50-90 (12 shards, every 4th row).
- **Result (stated minus realized, points, 95% bootstrap):**

  | starts | n | stated | vs live | net vs itself | vs people model |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | all | 2,085 | 60.8 | +6.3 [5.0, 7.6] | +6.5 [5.2, 7.8] | +5.9 [4.6, 7.1] |
  | all, bot to move | 1,042 | 60.9 | +4.5 [2.8, 6.2] | +4.3 [2.4, 5.9] | +3.7 [2.0, 5.3] |
  | all, person to move | 1,043 | 60.8 | +8.0 [6.1, 9.8] | +8.8 [7.1, 10.6] | +8.0 [6.2, 9.8] |
  | 2100+, bot to move | 293 | 57.1 | +6.6 [3.4, 9.8] | +4.9 [1.6, 8.3] | +2.1 [-1.3, 5.4] |
  | 2100+, person to move | 304 | 53.1 | +12.0 [8.7, 15.5] | +9.6 [6.4, 12.9] | +8.3 [5.1, 11.6] |

  By stated chance (all starts; stated / live / vs itself): 30% → 15 / 21; 51% → 31 / 28; 71% → 55 / 55; 96% → 93 / 92. Self-play control, own positions, turns 50-90: stated 62.3, won 62.8, gap −0.5 [−1.1, +0.1] (8,307 rows, 488 games): calibrated.
- **Reading:** almost all of the late overconfidence on people's positions comes back when the net plays those positions against itself (+6.5 vs +6.3 live; at 2100+ with the bot to move +4.9 vs +6.6). So it is mostly the positions, not people's late play: people steer games into states the net has rarely trained on, and it overrates the bot's side there. Production's people starts are cut at turns 16-60 (starts.py MIN/MAX_TURN), so late people positions are rare in training. Half the gap sits where the person is to move; the net is only asked from the mover's chair in search, so that half matters for training data more than for play. **E82's caveat:** E82 picked games that ran 70+ rolls, which picks future losses (people win the long games), so its +14-17 overstates the gap; E84 has no such selection.
- **Step 1 (PROPOSED, needs the lead's OK for actor time):** add late people starts (turns 50-90, training split only; the holdout stays out) to a fine-tune of round 189: about 20,000 self-play games from those starts with the best net (e54-vm after E83, about 2 min of play), mixed with one production window, 2 epochs at production's lr, on the PC. **Metric:** this yardstick with the bot to move (+4.3 vs itself, +4.5 live) should fall under +1.5 while the self-play control stays within ±1; 2100+ holdout late error (round 189 0.397) must not rise; then the gate vs round 189 (2,000 games). **If it works,** the production change is training's (the starts' turn range), not research's. **Cost:** about an hour of GPU and the actor VM's time (cents); no fleet unless the gate is reached. **Revert:** nothing deployed.
- **Prep (Oct 21, 4:20 PM):** the late starts file is being built ahead of the OK (data/orchestrator/e84/late_starts.jsonl.gz, starts.py --min-turn 50 --max-turn 90, training games only). Self-play needs no new code: selfplay.py --human-starts that file --human-start-rate 1.0. Built 4:15 PM: 125,176 starting positions from 4,427 games.
- **Step 1 RUNNING (lead's GO 9 PM; started Oct 21, 9:50 PM Toronto):** jobs/e84-run.sh (scratch e84_run.py), log jobs/e84.log, pid jobs/e84.pid. Waits for E85 to leave the PC, then holds gpu.lock (e84-late) through: (a) 20,000 games of round 189 vs itself from the late starts with production's self-play search (400 simulations on a quarter of moves, 24 otherwise): 16,000 on the PC's 12 idle cores (it is out of the fleet anyway) and 4,000 on the coordinator at niceness 19; a 16-game trial took 12 s a game per worker on the busy coordinator, so about 3-4 h; (b) fine-tune of round 189 as production (2 epochs, lr 3e-4, EMA 0.9997, 4M drawn) on self-play rounds 182-199, people's value rows ×12 and the late games ×1, seed 84. Fleet not used: its cores are full with production, so niceness 19 there would get almost nothing. This stretches the PC's outage to about 3 AM. Output data/orchestrator/e84/net.npz. Then: e84_cal.py (bot to move, r189 vs the new net), e84_sp.py control, 2100+ late error, and on a pass 2,000 games vs round 189.
- **Step 1 FAILED (Oct 22, 7:50 AM Toronto, research):** games done 1:01 AM (coordinator, 4,000) and 6:34 AM (PC, 16,000; 313 shards); fine-tune 6:38-6:46 AM (42,063 games, 3.80M train / 196k val; val policy 0.747, value 0.378), gpu.lock released 6:46 AM. Checks, round 189 → E84 net:
  - yardstick, E71's 2,085 holdout starts, bot to move (n 1,042): live gap **+5.1 → +4.9** (95% +3.2 to +6.5), vs-itself +4.8 → +4.6. Line was under +1.5: **fail**. 2100+ bot to move +6.8 → +6.8.
  - 2100+ holdout (243 games): late 0.397 → **0.395**, all 0.619 → 0.620 (paired +0.0007, −0.003 to +0.005). Level.
  - self-play control (round 213, 12 shards, turns 50-90): empty board −0.1 → −0.3; people starts −2.8 → −2.9. Unchanged.
  - round 189 on the E84 late games themselves (24 shards, 18,881 rows, 1,506 games): **+0.1 [−0.3, +0.5]**, already calibrated; the E84 net the same.
- **Reading:** the training data had nothing to correct on average. The value is zero-sum, so the bot's-chair overconfidence (+4.8 vs itself from E71's starts) means the person's chair is under-rated by the same amount; self-play rows from both chairs cancel. The net misjudges *which side* is better in people's late positions (bot-style holdings over-rated, people's under-rated), and an unweighted fine-tune on more of the same positions does not move it. Not proven yet: the late games' rows split by the start's bot colour should show about +5 / −5 (needs the start index per game; no GPU). No match played (stop before the gate). **Revert:** nothing deployed. PC free for training since 6:46 AM.
- **Split by chair (Oct 22, 8:45 AM Toronto, research, no GPU):** E84's late games, each row given the colour the bot had in the live game the start came from (start index from the game's seed as selfplay.human_start draws it; bot colour from the capture; the first row's colour by matching the start position's encoding, clear in 5,105 of 5,111 games). Round 189:
  - the start position only (80 shards, 5,107 games; the first row is the mover's): bot's chair to move **+3.4 [+2.4, +4.6]** (n 2,479), person's chair to move **−1.3 [−2.7, −0.2]** (n 2,628).
  - every 4th row, turns 50-90 (24 shards, 19,433 rows, about 1,465 games): bot's chair **+1.5 [+1.0, +2.1]**, person's chair **−0.8 [−1.4, −0.2]**. The E84 net: +1.4 / −1.0, the same.
- **Reading:** the guess of +5 / −5 is only half right. The one-sided error is real but sits on the start position itself: about two thirds of the live +5.1 with the bot to move comes back at the start (+3.4), and it fades to +1.5 once the net's own play has moved the game on. A start is 1 row in about 25 of its game, so the fine-tune barely saw it. Step 2 idea (to the lead, not run): weight the start rows (both chairs) so they are a set share of the batch (about 10%), same yardstick and pass lines. **Training's starts.py turn-range change: none to test yet**; step 1 shows that more late starts at production's weights do not move the error. Script: scratch/research/e84_chair.py NET [SHARDS] [first].

## E85 — research: E54's step 50 with its value head retrained on production's data (owner's yes Oct 21, 7:58 PM Toronto, to decision e54-value-from-production; step 1 WAITING for the PC)
- **Why:** E54's policy-gradient training drifts the value head on people's games: round 73 (its start) 0.610 on the 2100+ holdout, step 10 0.635, step 30-60 0.646, late 0.424 vs round 189's 0.397 (E83). The value there learns from GAE returns on its own pool games. Production's value learns from self-play plus people's games and reads 0.619.
- **Step 1 (what):** step 50 (data/orchestrator/e54/run1/iter-50/net.npz) plus 1 epoch of train.py with the policy loss off (--policy-weight 0, new, landed c78b95c) and a KL of 0.02 to step 50's own policy, so the shared layers can move but the policy is held. Data: production's last window (self-play rounds 182-199) plus people's value rows 12 times (as production's HUMAN_REPEAT), 4M positions drawn (the PC's 9 GB can't take 12M), lr 3e-5, no EMA, value mix 0.5 (production's), seed 85. About 15 min of GPU plus copying.
- **Where:** the PC's GPU under gpu.lock. The PC's 10 self-play workers leave all but about 1 GB free, so the job waits until training takes the PC out of workers.env (asked Oct 21, 8:10 PM; job waiting since 8:10 PM, pid in jobs/e85.pid) and then starts by itself. **PC out 8:13 PM** (training removed both entries from workers.env; no stream was on the PC then, round 198 was training). Training puts them back when research says E85 is done. **PC back Oct 22, 8:00 AM** (E85 and E84 step 1 both done; e84.log shows gpu.lock released 6:46 AM; both entries re-inserted in workers.env, they take effect from round 214's self-play on). Run: jobs/e85-run.sh (scratch e85_value.py), log jobs/e85.log, net to data/orchestrator/e85/net.npz.
- **Checks after step 1:** 2100+ holdout (human2100.py, paired, 243 games) vs step 50 and round 189; top-move agreement with step 50 on validation positions, at least 95%; then 2,000 games at 200 simulations vs step 50 and 2,000 vs round 189 (fleet time, about 35 min, from the lead).
- **Stop:** 2100+ error not at 0.630 or lower, or under 50% vs step 50. **Pass:** 0.630 or lower and at least 54% vs round 189. Step 2 (only on a pass): the E54 loop for 10 steps from step 50 with the policy-gradient value weight 0 and production rows for the value, on a spot VM (about 1.5 h, about US$0.30).
- **Cost:** no money for step 1; about 1 h of the PC (its self-play share) and 35 min of fleet. **Revert:** nothing in production changes; when done, tell training the PC is free.
- **FAILED on the first try (Oct 21, 8:18 PM Toronto; lead's reading 8:45 PM):** the PC got the data at 8:13-8:18 PM, but its copy of train.py is older than c78b95c, so it rejected `--policy-weight 0 --policy-kl 0.02` ("unrecognized arguments") and the job died fetching a net that was never written (rsync exit 23, jobs/e85.log). The PC is still out of workers.env. Research syncs the code to the PC (its checkout or an rsync of catanatron/), reruns the job, then tells training. No change to the design.
- **RERUN (Oct 21, 9:46 PM Toronto, research):** e85_value.py now copies catanatron/ and pyproject.toml to the PC first (as sync_workers.sh does) and checks that the PC's train.py lists both flags before copying data; it did (9:46 PM). Training started 9:50 PM. Checks (holdout, top-move agreement) run by themselves when the net lands (jobs/e85-after.log). E84 step 1 follows on the same PC outage, then research tells training the PC is free.
- **Step 1 DONE, offline checks PASS (Oct 21, 9:55-10:05 PM Toronto, research):** net data/orchestrator/e85/net.npz (40,920 games, 3.8M train / 199k val positions; val policy 0.983, value 0.412). 2100+ holdout (243 games, 9,562 positions, e85/holdout.txt): **0.623** (pass line 0.630); step 50 0.647, round 189 0.619. Paired: e85 − step 50 −0.025 (95% −0.037 to −0.011), e85 − r189 +0.004 (−0.007 to +0.015). Late 0.414 (step 50 0.424, r189 0.397): value retraining wins back about 40% of step 50's late gap, not all. Top move same as step 50 on 97.2% of 10,386 people's positions (line 95%). PC handed straight to E84 (lock e84-late from 9:56 PM).

## E84 — step 2 GO: the start rows weighted up (lead, Oct 22, 8:55 AM Toronto)
- **Why:** step 1 failed as research found (bot to move +5.1 → +4.9, late error level): more late self-play at production's weights moves nothing. The split by chair says why: the one-sided error sits on the start position itself (bot's chair +3.4 too sure, person's chair −1.3) and fades to +1.5 once the net's own play has moved on. A start is one row in about 25 of its game, so the fine-tune barely saw it.
- **What:** the same fine-tune of round 189, with each late game's start rows (both chairs) repeated into extra shards so they make about 10% of the batch. Data only, no code. About 30 min of the PC's GPU under gpu.lock; research asks training for the window and tells it when the PC is free.
- **Pass:** E84's yardstick with the bot to move under +1.5, the self-play control within ±1, 2100+ late error not above 0.397; then 2,000 games vs round 189 at 200 simulations (fleet time granted). **Revert:** nothing deployed.
- **Step 2 WAITING for the PC (Oct 22, 8:45 AM Toronto, research):** start rows built: the first row of each of E84's 19,958 late games (half bot's chair to move, half person's), in data/orchestrator/e84/starts/ (313 small shards, 8.6 MB; train.py reads them, checked on CPU: round 189's value loss on them 0.276). The pool without them is 17.9M positions (rounds 182-199, people's value rows ×12, the late games ×1), so the starts folder is listed 100 times: 10.0% of the pool, each start row drawn about 20 times an epoch (40 over the run; validation, split by game, will show if they are memorised). Otherwise step 1's run exactly: 2 epochs, lr 3e-4, EMA 0.9997, 4M drawn, seed 84. Job jobs/e84s2-run.sh (scratch e84_step2.py), log jobs/e84s2.log, pid jobs/e84s2.pid; it waits for training to take the PC out of workers.env, then holds gpu.lock (e84-starts), about 30 min; net to data/orchestrator/e84/step2/net.npz. Asked training for the window at a round boundary.
- **Step 2 FAILED (Oct 22, 12:00 PM Toronto, research):** fine-tune ran 9:37-10:32 AM (39,484 games, 3.80M train / 201k val; val policy 0.757, value 0.387; gpu.lock released 10:32 AM). Checks, round 189 → step 2 net:
  - yardstick, E71's 2,085 holdout starts, bot to move (n 1,042): live gap +5.1 → **+4.6** (95% +2.9 to +6.3); line under +1.5: **fail**. vs-itself +4.8 → +4.3. 2100+ bot to move +6.8 → +6.3.
  - 2100+ holdout (243 games): late 0.397 → **0.396**, all 0.619 → 0.618 (paired −0.0015, −0.006 to +0.003). Level.
  - the start rows themselves (E84's late games, 24 shards, first row, 1,531 games), which the run drew about 40 times each: bot's chair +3.4 → **+3.0** [+0.9, +5.3], person's chair −1.3 → **−2.0** [−4.5, +0.4]. Not fixed even in training's own rows.
- **Reading:** the net cannot see which chair is the bot's from the position, and over both chairs the start rows are already calibrated (+0.1), so there is nothing for training to correct. Research's best explanation: the optimizer's curse. The bot reaches these positions by searching for states its value rates highly, so the positions it chose are, on average, the ones it over-rates; against itself both sides are picked that way and the errors cancel, against people only the bot's side is. More or reweighted data of the same kind cannot fix this. No match played (stopped before the gate). **E84 CLOSED.** **Revert:** nothing deployed.

## E88 — research: the person's chair played by the people model from people's late positions (lead's research item, Oct 22, 8:55 AM Toronto; ORDERED after E84 step 2, measurement first)
- **Source:** E84's split by chair (our data), and AlphaStar's league (Vinyals et al., Nature 2019), where agents seeded from human data gave self-play what self-play alone lacked.
- **Why:** self-play labels from people's late positions are calibrated against self-play (+0.1 on the E84 games), so they cannot correct the live gap; the gap exists because people play the person's chair differently from the net. E81's fixed people model (fit on people's moves, 71% of them matched) in the person's chair may give labels closer to live, with no more live games.
- **What:** about 2,000 games from E84's late starts (turns 50-90, training split): the best net in the chair the bot had live, the people model (data/orchestrator/e81/people_net.npz) in the person's chair, 200 simulations each. Read the bot's chair's win rate against (a) self-play's from the same starts (E84's games) and (b) the net's stated value. If the match tool cannot hold the people model to one chair per start, play both assignments and read only the games with the net in the bot's chair.
- **Metric:** the bot's chair wins at least 2 points less against the people model than in self-play from the same starts (the live direction): the labels carry the correction, and step 2 is a value fine-tune on those games with E84's yardstick (bot to move under +1.5, late error not above 0.397). As much or more: dead in one match.
- **Cost:** about 10 min of fleet time. **Revert:** nothing deployed.
- **CLOSED, answered by E71's games, dead by its own rule (research, Oct 22, 9:00 AM Toronto).** No match played. E71 step 0 (Oct 21, 8:41-9:00 AM) is this match: round 73 in the bot's chair from 2,085 people's late positions, one game a start, same seeds, against data/orchestrator/e68/people_net.npz (byte for byte the same file as e81/people_net.npz) and against itself. The bot's chair won 55.0% ± 2.1 vs the people model, 54.3% ± 2.1 vs itself, live 54.5% ± 2.1. Paired by start, self-play minus people model −0.7 ± 1.5 points (2100+, 597 starts: −2.0 ± 2.8). The rule needed the people model to cost the bot 2+ points; it costs nothing, so its labels equal self-play's. Caveats: round 73 not the best net, E71's starts not E84's turns 50-90; a rerun is 10 min of fleet with e71_late.py if wanted. Reading: E84's +3.4 bot-to-move error at the start rows is measured against self-play outcomes from those starts, so the net is miscalibrated on people's positions against its own play; that is what E84 step 2 (start rows at 10%) tests, and no people-like opponent is needed for it.

## E90 — research: the E71 yardstick from people's openings (research, Oct 22, 6:40 PM Toronto; PROPOSED, waits for the lead's fleet OK)
- **Why:** E71 found that from people's late (turns 50-90) and mid (15-40) positions, every net plays out the game against the people model about as well as live, and no net does worse against people than against itself. So the bot's live losses are already decided by turn 15. This test asks whether they are decided by the placements themselves, or in turns 1-15.
- **What:** the same four arms and seeds as E71 (round 73 vs the people model, round 73 vs itself, round 152 vs the people model, E54 step 30 vs the people model, 200 simulations, one game a start), from 949 starts: every holdout game cut at its first roll, right after the four placements (e71/starts_open.jsonl, built Oct 22, 6:35 PM; 284 against 2100+ people, 436 under 2100, 229 unrated). Live result from these starts: bot 58.3% (553 of 949).
- **Reading:** (1) the bot's chair score vs live 58.3%: if self-play from people's placements wins about what live did, the placements decide the result and the rest of the game is played as well as live; if it wins 3+ points more than live, the bot loses ground to people in turns 1-15. (2) Same-as-live share: at turn 50 it was ~70%; near 50% here would mean the placements carry little. (3) Paired E54 minus round 73: E71 found +2.0 late and +2.8 mid; if it is still about +3 here, E54's edge is spread evenly and an opening yardstick adds nothing; if it is larger, the opening is where E54 gains. (4) vs itself minus vs people: whether the people model plays the early game better than the bot.
- **Games:** 4 × 949 = 3,796 on the fleet at nice 19, about 40-50 min (step 1 played 9,700 shorter games in 70 min). ±3.1 points per arm, about ±2.5 paired.
- **Job:** jobs/e90-run.sh (launch.py e90, log jobs/e90.log); games to e71/open-*.jsonl, paired lines by e71_pair.py open-. **Cost:** fleet time only. **Revert:** nothing deployed.

## E91 — research: why is E54 step 50 weak against OpenHex's engines and strong against people? (owner's question Oct 22, 6:44 PM, via the lead 6:55 PM; RUNNING since 6:48 PM Toronto; note due by the 9 PM report)
- **Facts:** OpenHex step 50 11-22 after 33 games (first seat vs catanrl-1v1-search 5-17; round 152 there 22-13 first, 52/100 overall). Colonist: step 50 69-37 vs step 30's 57-60 on the same VM and hours (same band +15.1 [+1.6, +28.4]). Offline 54.0% vs round 189 and 56% vs round 152 at 200 sims.
- **Checks (nothing deployed):** (1) pool overfitting: top-move agreement and prior distance of step 50 with round 73 and round 208 on self-play (round 208's data), people's (holdout policy_won, opponent_exact) and OpenHex positions (all saved games replayed with catanatron.openhex.follow, our decisions with 2+ moves, by phase); round 208 vs round 73 as the yardstick. (2) value: each net's error to the result by phase on OpenHex positions, calibration by bin, peak win chance in lost games by the net that played. (3) seat: step 50 vs round 208, 1,000 games at 200 sims on the fleet (granted, about 8 min), seed 9100000000.
- **Jobs:** jobs/e91seat-run.sh (log jobs/e91seat.log, lines to e91/seat.txt); jobs/e91drift-run.sh (scratch/research/e91_drift.py, coordinator CPU, log jobs/e91drift.log).
- **RESULT (Oct 22, 7:10 PM Toronto):**
  - **Seat (1,000 games, 200 sims, 10 min of fleet):** step 50 beat round 208 **56.2% ± 2.9** (562-438; think time equal, 296 vs 293 ms a move). The first seat won 54.4% of games, so step 50 won about 61% going first and 52% going second (±4.3 each). Offline step 50 is, if anything, better going first. The OpenHex first-seat collapse is not a property of the net in our engine.
  - **Drift (raw policy, top move):** step 50 vs round 73 85.4% (self-play, 249,423 positions), 83.3% (people, 44,861), 85.8% (OpenHex, 28,303); round 208 vs round 73 85.6 / 85.2 / 85.1; step 50 vs round 208 80.4 / 79.7 / 80.6. OpenHex by phase (under 20, 20-49, 50+): step 50 vs round 73 84.1 / 86.8 / 85.7. So step 50 has moved as far from round 73 as production has, in another direction, and **no more on OpenHex positions than anywhere else**. Pool overfitting would show as low agreement where it loses; it doesn't.
  - **Value on OpenHex positions (301 games replayed, 10 out of sync and skipped):** error to the result, step 50 0.590, round 208 0.580, round 152 0.579, round 73 0.565; step 50 minus round 208 +0.010 (±0.015 by game); turn 50+ 0.360 vs 0.359 (+0.001 ±0.014). Every net is over-sure on OpenHex the same way (the middle bin "says 51%" wins 37-42%). Peak win chance in lost games: median 81% for all three nets that played (60%+ in 87-90% of losses). Step 50's value is not the problem there.
  - **Where step 50's OpenHex games go wrong:** in its 27 first-seat games, at turn 50+ every net reads its positions at about 51% (round 152's first-seat games: 67%), and it won 26%. So it reaches worse middle games and then converts 25 points under its own reading (round 73 and round 152 convert within 0-9 points). 27 games: about ±17 points.
  - **Reading:** (1) pool overfitting: not supported (agreement the same everywhere). (3) seat: not the net (offline first seat is its better seat). (4) value: not supported on OpenHex (same error, same over-sureness, same peaks as round 152 and round 73). (2) rules: the one difference left untested is the dice. **OpenHex rolls plain dice; colonist and all our training use balanced dice.** Step 50 learned by playing out 1M+ games on balanced dice with no search; a policy that leans on the deck (when a 7 is due, which numbers are spent) gains on colonist and offline and loses where the dice are plain. E34 found round 73 insensitive to plain dice; step 50 was never checked. Also possible: catanrl-1v1-search changed between round 152's games (Oct 20-22) and step 50's (Oct 22); E87 on round 208 would show that. 11-24 over 35 games vs round 152's 52/100 is about 2 standard errors.
  - **For Tedi4960:** colonist uses balanced dice and people; every measure in those conditions favours step 50 (live same band +15.1 [+1.6, +28.4]; offline 56% vs round 208, 54% vs round 189). Keep step 50; E83's verdict at 300 games stands. OpenHex is no evidence against it for the season push unless the plain-dice test below shows the edge is the dice deck, and even then colonist's dice are the balanced deck.
  - **Proposed test (about 20 min of fleet):** step 50 vs round 208 at 200 sims, 2,000 games, plain dice (evaluate's random-dice rule), seat split. Reading: step 50 at 52% or lower (4+ points under today's 56.2%) means its edge rides on the balanced deck and explains OpenHex; still about 56% means the cause is on OpenHex's side (opponent or client), and E87 reads it. Caveat: in our engine plain dice give the nets an empty dice input (E34: further from training than what the OpenHex client feeds), so this tests "plain dice, as our engine plays them"; both nets get the same input, so the paired difference still reads. Job ready, NOT launched: jobs/e91dice-run.sh (launch.py e91dice; gate.py now takes --plain-dice; lines to e91/dice.txt), seed 9110000000.
- **Plain-dice RESULT (Oct 22, 9:01 PM Toronto; 2,000 games, 200 sims, 16 min of fleet):** step 50 beat round 208 **57.9% ± 2.1** on plain dice (1158-842; first seat won 50.5%; think time equal, 250 vs 248 ms a move). Against 56.2% ± 2.9 on the balanced deck, so its edge does **not** ride on the dice deck. With the board ruled out, the net's seat, drift and value ruled out, what is left of the OpenHex gap is OpenHex's side: mostly the opponent (catanrl-1v1-search, where the whole gap sits going first) or the client. E87 (round 208 against the same opponent) reads it; research has nothing more to test offline without catanrl's code. **E91 CLOSED** (Oct 22, 9:15 PM): step 50 is not weak in our engine under either dice rule; Tedi4960 keeps it.
- **Rules check with openhex's facts (Oct 22, 7:30 PM Toronto):** OpenHex's rules match ours except (a) plain dice, (b) the board's numbers always follow the official spiral, (c) only a knight before the roll, (d) a 60 s clock that aborts. (b) is **ruled out**: our matches build every game with `rules.new_game`, whose default is the official spiral (colonist's 228 recorded boards follow it too), so the 1,000-game seat match was already on OpenHex-like boards. (c) touches few positions. That leaves two candidates: the **dice** (the test above) and the **opponent**: catanrl-1v1-search played 27 of step 50's 36 games, and the whole gap is going first against it (step 50 5-18, round 152 22-13; 23 and 35 games, a difference of about 41 points with a ±25 range). Its style comes from Catanatron's hand-made value bot, not from people or from our pool. If the dice test comes back about 56%, the next reading is E87 (round 208 against the same opponent); a direct offline test against catanrl itself would need its code, which we don't have installed (a package decision for the owner), so it is not proposed.

## E92 — research: E85 step 1 through the production gate against round 208 (lead's research item, Oct 22, 8:50 PM Toronto; ORDERED, runs tonight after E91's dice test; about 1 h of the fleet)
- **Why:** production kept round 208 for 22 rounds (r209-r230) at 49.6-50.7% over 4,000-8,000 games each: every candidate is a re-fit of the same net on the same window. The E54 line is the only thing that beats production's best offline (step 50 56.2% ± 2.9 against round 208 at 200 simulations, E91) and the leading account plays it live (Tedi4960 66% since Oct 21, 7:45 PM, 2209 to 2405). The gate's own four checks say whether the line can be production's starting point. Source: our gate history and E91's seat match; no outside paper claims more than this.
- **What:** E85 step 1's net (data/orchestrator/e85/net.npz: step 50 with its value head retrained on production's data; 2100+ people error 0.623 against step 50's 0.647) through the same checks loop.py applies to a round, against round 208: (1) the match with the gate's SPRT (50% against 54%, up to 8,000 games, 200 simulations); (2) the anchor (round 13, 2,000 games, a drop of at most 0.02 from round 208's 0.668); (3) the people-model benchmark against round 208's 57.4%; (4) live value error on the same 37,000 positions, at most 0.01 above round 208's 0.559. Training supplies the exact settings to research.
- **Pass:** all four. Then the lead decides, together with E89's 60-game read on Finlay3621, whether production restarts from E85 step 1 as best at a safe point (training, safe_restart_training.sh) so the accounts that follow promotions take it. Fail on any check: the E54 line stays on Tedi4960 and Finlay3621 only; research's next step is E85 step 3 with that check as the target.
- **Cost:** about 1 h of the fleet at nice 19, no money. **Revert:** nothing deployed by this experiment.
- **Started (research, Oct 22, 8:55 PM Toronto):** queued behind e91dice, then E90 follows (all at nice 19). Settings taken from train.sh and loop.py as they run now, since training's message had not come: the match is **not SPRT any more**, it is the "confidence" gate: 4,000 games at 200 sims (stopped after 1,000 if the top of the 95% range is under 50.5%), a near miss (bottom of the range under 50%, score 50.4% or more) plays 4,000 more on other boards; **pass = bottom of the 95% range at 50% or more** (about 51.6% over 8,000). **Corrected by training (Oct 22, 9:10 PM Toronto): production runs the "progress" gate.** Same games and futility stop, but it passes at a plain score of 51% or more; 50.4-52% after 4,000 plays 4,000 more and is judged at 51% over 8,000. The running job keeps the confidence wording in its log; research reads its numbers by the progress rule (if 4,000 games land at 51.6-52%, research plays the extra 4,000 itself at seed 9,200,750,000). Benchmark AB:2 (400 games) is in production's list but its guard is 1.0, so it never blocks; run after if the fleet has room. Anchor: round 13, 2,000 games, on the loop's own anchor boards (the ones round 208 scored 0.668 on), floor 0.648. People model: e81/people_net.npz, 4,000 games on the loop's own boards, floor 0.5543. Live: data/human/holdout/value, MSE at most 0.01 over round 208's on the same positions, plus the gate's paired by-game check (bottom of the range of the MSE and accuracy gain not under -0.01). Not run: the private tactical check and the AB:2 reading (it never blocks). Match boards are new (seed 9,200,000,000). Lines to e92/gate.txt.
- **Layout (Oct 22, 9:15 PM Toronto, training's question):** E85 step 1 has exactly the production net's layout: the same 54 arrays with the same shapes and types as round 208 (graph net, hidden 32, global 64, 3 layers, 102,378 weights), so production can train on from it.
- **Check 4, live error (9:02 PM):** passes. On the 37,400 positions as they are now, E85 step 1 0.5601 and round 208 0.5601 (recomputed on the same set); by game the difference is 0.0000 (95% -0.005 to +0.005). Match, anchor and people checks running.

## E88 — rerun on step 50 (late-game, lead's order Oct 22, 8:50 PM Toronto; CLOSED Oct 22, 9:30 PM: dead)
- **Why a rerun:** E88 was closed at 9 AM on round 73's E71 games (people model costs the bot's chair −0.7 ± 1.5 points, the wrong way). E79's round 208 games (robber starts) read the same way (people model +0.9 and +1.2 for the bot). The lead wants it on the net the top account plays. Expected: dead again.
- **What:** E54 step 50 (data/orchestrator/e54/run1/iter-50/net.npz, Tedi4960's net) in the bot's chair from E71's 2,085 late holdout starts (people's games, turns 50-90; the starts E84's yardstick uses, each with its live result), once against the people model (data/orchestrator/e68/people_net.npz) and once against itself, 200 simulations, same seeds (paired by start). research's e71_late.py, fleet at nice 19 alongside E92. Job jobs/e88-run.sh, log jobs/e88.log, games data/orchestrator/e88/.
- **Metric (unchanged):** the bot's chair wins at least 2 points less against the people model than against itself, paired by start (all and 2100+); then step 2 (a value fine-tune on those games). Otherwise dead. Also read: each arm against the live result from the same starts.
- **Cost:** 4,170 games, about 20-30 min of fleet at nice 19. **Revert:** nothing deployed.
- **Result (Oct 22, 9:27 PM; 4,170 games, 9:06-9:27 PM at nice 19 next to E92):** the bot's chair won **56.5%** against the people model and **54.1%** against itself from the same 2,085 starts; paired by start, people model minus self **+2.4 [+0.9, +4.0]** (2100+: +1.8 [−1.3, +5.0], n 597; <2100: +2.4 [+0.3, +4.5]; 2300+: +3.5 [−8.5, +15.5], n 57). The wrong direction, as on round 73 (E71) and round 208 (E79): the people model is an easier person than the net itself, so its labels would make the value more over-sure, not less. **Dead.** Worth noting: from the 2100+ starts the real people beat the bot's chair more than either stand-in (live 45.7% vs self-play 48.1% vs people model 49.9%); the missing strength is the strong person's, which only real games against strong people carry. Paired read: data/orchestrator/scratch/late-game/e88_read.py.

## E93 — late-game: E85 step 1's value pass with our live games against 2100+ people weighted up (fix 1 of the late-game note; GPU window from research for Oct 23, 7 AM Toronto; QUEUED, waits for 7 AM and the PC)
- **Why:** against 2100+ people the bot states more than it wins, and the gap grows with the person's rating and with every turn (analysis/late-2026-10-22-peak-losses.md: +3 / +10 / +15 points at bot turns 0 / 10 / 25 against 2100-2300, +9 / +15 / +22 against 2300+). E84 (self-play labels) and E88 (people-model labels) cannot carry it; real results against strong people are the only labels that do. Today they are about 5% of what E85 step 1's value pass draws (people's rows are about 15%, and two thirds of those are under 2100).
- **Baseline (Oct 22, 9:45 PM, scratch/late-game/e93_eval.py, people holdout split, 318 games against 2100+, 12,518 positions; the holdout is split by capture, never trained on):** stated minus won, 2100+: E85 step 1 **+8.2 [+4.7, +11.9]**, value error 0.613; step 50 +10.6, 0.636. Under 2100 (606 games): E85 step 1 +2.3, 0.545.
- **What:** research's e85_value.py recipe unchanged (step 50, policy loss off, KL 0.02 to its own policy, 1 epoch, lr 3e-5, 4M positions drawn, rounds 182-199 + people's value rows ×12, seed 85), plus the training split's 1,932 games against 2100+ people (76,017 positions, list in e93/train2100.txt) listed 28 more times: about 16% of the draw instead of about 5%, each such row seen about 8 times. E85 step 1 is the paired control (same start and recipe). Job scratch/late-game/e93_value.py (jobs/e93-run.sh, log jobs/e93.log): waits for 7 AM Toronto, then for the PC to be out of workers.env with 6 GB free (as E85), holds gpu.lock (tag e93-value). About 15 min of GPU plus copying. Net data/orchestrator/e93/net.npz.
- **Pass (against E85 step 1, paired by game on the holdout):** (1) 2100+ stated minus won falls by at least 4 points (to +4.2 or less), paired range clear of zero; (2) 2100+ value error not higher (paired upper bound under +0.005); (3) under 2100, value error not more than 0.005 higher and the gap not below −3 (no over-correction); (4) 2,000 games at 200 simulations against E85 step 1, at least 49% (fleet, about 20 min, asked of the lead after (1)-(3) pass). **Stop:** any of (1)-(3) fails: dead, nothing more run.
- **Risk:** it may lower the stated number without changing moves; only live play tells. Any live use is the lead's decision (season freeze: nothing new on the top three accounts from Oct 25 if the season ends Oct 27).
- **Cost:** about 30 min of the PC (its self-play share, as E85), 20 min of fleet on a pass, no money. **Revert:** nothing deployed; remove gpu.lock if the job dies holding it; tell research and training the PC is free.
